# Daily Summary: 2025-04-02

### Towards Physically Plausible Video Generation via VLM Planning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23368v1)
- **Authors**: Xindi Yang, Baolu Li, Yiming Zhang, Zhenfei Yin, Lei Bai, Liqian Ma, Zhiyong Wang, Jianfei Cai, Tien-Tsin Wong, Huchuan Lu, Xu Jia
- **Abstract**: Video diffusion models (VDMs) have advanced significantly in recent years, enabling the generation of highly realistic videos and drawing the attention of the community in their potential as world simulators. However, despite their capabilities, VDMs often fail to produce physically plausible videos due to an inherent lack of understanding of physics, resulting in incorrect dynamics and event sequences. To address this limitation, we propose a novel two-stage image-to-video generation framework that explicitly incorporates physics. In the first stage, we employ a Vision Language Model (VLM) as a coarse-grained motion planner, integrating chain-of-thought and physics-aware reasoning to predict a rough motion trajectories/changes that approximate real-world physical dynamics while ensuring the inter-frame consistency. In the second stage, we use the predicted motion trajectories/changes to guide the video generation of a VDM. As the predicted motion trajectories/changes are rough, noise is added during inference to provide freedom to the VDM in generating motion with more fine details. Extensive experimental results demonstrate that our framework can produce physically plausible motion, and comparative evaluations highlight the notable superiority of our approach over existing methods. More video results are available on our Project Page: https://madaoer.github.io/projects/physically_plausible_video_generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Towards Physically Plausible Video Generation via VLM Planning":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of generating physically plausible videos using Video Diffusion Models (VDMs), which often struggle with accurate physics simulation.  The authors propose a two-stage framework.  First, a Vision Language Model (VLM) is used as a coarse-grained motion planner, leveraging chain-of-thought and physics-aware reasoning to predict approximate motion trajectories.  Second, a VDM is guided by these predicted trajectories, with added noise during inference to allow for fine-grained detail generation.  Experiments on physics-based video generation benchmarks demonstrate the superiority of their approach over existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of combining a VLM for high-level, physics-aware planning with a VDM for detailed video synthesis is relatively novel.  While individual components (VLMs, VDMs, motion control) are well-established, the explicit coupling to enforce physical plausibility is a worthwhile contribution.  The use of chain-of-thought reasoning within the VLM for motion planning also adds a layer of novelty.  However, the novelty lies more in the clever integration of existing techniques than in groundbreaking algorithmic breakthroughs.
*   **Significance:**  Generating physically plausible videos has significant implications for areas like robotics, simulation, and content creation.  By improving the physical realism of generated videos, this paper addresses a critical limitation of current VDMs. The benchmarks used are relevant and established. Quantitative and qualitative evaluations showcase significant improvement over current models. There is also a user study, highlighting how individuals assess physical plausibility and visual realism. The code provided is good, allowing for easy reproducibility for future researchers.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of VDMs in generating physically plausible videos.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** The two-stage framework is logical and well-explained.  The rationale for each component is clearly presented.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental results on established benchmarks (PhyGenBench, Physics-IQ) provide convincing evidence of the superiority of their method. Comprehensive ablation study highlighting the importance of individual contributions to the overall pipeline. A good qualitative study illustrating the advantage of the current pipeline for generating physically realistic videos.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The project page with code contributes to the reproducibility of their work.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The framework, as described, is limited by the ability of VLMs to reason about physics and by the expressiveness of bounding boxes for motion representation. Complex physical phenomena or object deformations that cannot be captured by bounding box movements are not addressed.
    *   **VLM Dependency:**  The system relies heavily on the VLM's "understanding" of physics, which might be superficial.  The paper acknowledges this dependency but doesn't thoroughly explore alternative approaches if VLM understanding is lacking.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Failure Cases:** While the paper shows improvements, a more in-depth analysis of failure cases would be valuable.  Where does the system still struggle, and why?
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While results are quantitatively significant, it is still an incremental advancement towards video generation. There may be other techniques that provide comparable or even better gains.
*   **Potential Influence:**  This paper is likely to influence research in video generation by highlighting the importance of incorporating explicit physics understanding.  It could inspire further work on better integrating VLMs and VDMs or on developing alternative methods for generating physically plausible videos.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid approach to a significant problem, demonstrating improved performance on relevant benchmarks. While the core novelty is incremental, the integration of VLMs and VDMs is well-executed and effective. The limitations are clearly defined, and the positive results justify its contribution to the field.

### FeRG-LLM : Feature Engineering by Reason Generation Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23371v1)
- **Authors**: Jeonghyun Ko, Gyeongyun Park, Donghoon Lee, Kyunam Lee
- **Abstract**: One of the key tasks in machine learning for tabular data is feature engineering. Although it is vital for improving the performance of models, it demands considerable human expertise and deep domain knowledge, making it labor-intensive endeavor. To address this issue, we propose a novel framework, \textbf{FeRG-LLM} (\textbf{Fe}ature engineering by \textbf{R}eason \textbf{G}eneration \textbf{L}arge \textbf{L}anguage \textbf{M}odels), a large language model designed to automatically perform feature engineering at an 8-billion-parameter scale. We have constructed two-stage conversational dialogues that enable language models to analyze machine learning tasks and discovering new features, exhibiting their Chain-of-Thought (CoT) capabilities. We use these dialogues to fine-tune Llama 3.1 8B model and integrate Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) to receive feedback improving quality of new features and the model's performance. Our experiments show that FeRG-LLM performs comparably to or better than Llama 3.1 70B on most datasets, while using fewer resources and achieving reduced inference time. It outperforms other studies in classification tasks and performs well in regression tasks. Moreover, since it does not rely on cloud-hosted LLMs like GPT-4 with extra API costs when generating features, it can be deployed locally, addressing security concerns.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "FeRG-LLM: Feature Engineering by Reason Generation Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FeRG-LLM, a novel framework for automating feature engineering for tabular data using large language models (LLMs).  FeRG-LLM is designed as an 8-billion-parameter model that performs feature engineering via a two-stage conversational dialogue process.  The first stage involves the LLM analyzing machine learning tasks and proposing new feature ideas, utilizing Chain-of-Thought capabilities. The second stage has the LLM generate Python code to implement the suggested features. The model is fine-tuned using Llama 3.1 and Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) to improve the quality of generated features. The authors evaluated FeRG-LLM on several classification and regression datasets, demonstrating performance comparable to or better than much larger models (70B parameters) and existing feature engineering approaches, while also offering improved inference speed and the ability to be deployed locally, addressing security concerns.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the application of a relatively small, fine-tuned LLM (Llama 3.1 8B) for automated feature engineering, coupled with a DPO approach for refining generated rationales and code. The two-stage dialogue approach for reasoning is also a beneficial innovation.  While using LLMs for feature engineering is not entirely new, prior work typically relies on large, cloud-hosted models (e.g., GPT-4). The focus on a smaller, deployable model trained through a specific dialogue structure addresses both resource constraints and data security issues, giving it a unique appeal.

* **Significance:** The significance stems from several factors:
    *   **Resource Efficiency:** Demonstrating competitive performance with a much smaller model has significant implications for accessibility and deployment costs. This makes feature engineering more feasible for organizations with limited resources.
    *   **Data Security:**  The ability to run FeRG-LLM locally avoids the need to send sensitive data to external cloud services, addressing a crucial concern for many industries.
    *   **Automation:** The paper describes a nearly fully automated pipeline for feature engineering, potentially reducing the need for manual effort and specialized expertise. By generating executable code, it can also help to identify the overlooked patterns and boost overall efficiency.

* **Strengths:**
    *   The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of FeRG-LLM across a variety of datasets and tasks (classification and regression).
    *   The ablation studies provide strong evidence for the contributions of both the reasoning and DPO components.
    *   The discussion of inference speed highlights a practical advantage over larger models.
    *   The qualitative analysis of the generated code and rationales provides insights into the model's reasoning process and demonstrates improvement through DPO.
    *   The clear explanations of the experimental setup and evaluation metrics lend credibility to the findings.

* **Weaknesses:**
    *   The quantitative analysis of the trade-off between the computational overhead and performance gains in a two-step chain-of-thought and DPO training process is missing.
    *   The sensitivity of performance to the specific prompt design and hyperparameter settings of the fine-tuning and DPO processes is not thoroughly explored.
    *   Although local deployment is mentioned, the feasibility of implementing and maintaining this framework in real-world organizational processes could be investigated further.
    *   There is no comparison of the economic costs for utilizing the 8B model vs. the 70B model, including the hardware and electricity costs.
    *   In relation to the first two limitations, the paper focuses on how to make decisions related to model output and rationales, and does not appear to be examining the sensitivity of model decisions in the inputs, for example the impact of tabular data quality.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by demonstrating that effective automated feature engineering can be achieved with smaller, more accessible LLMs. It could stimulate further research into fine-tuning techniques and dialogue-based approaches for specific downstream tasks, as well as promote the development of more secure and resource-efficient machine learning solutions.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the identified strengths and weaknesses, and considering its novelty and potential significance, a score of 7 is justified. The paper offers a valuable contribution by presenting a practical and effective framework for automated feature engineering that addresses key concerns regarding resource constraints and data security. While there is room for improvement in terms of analyzing sensitivity and conducting a quantitative analysis for tradeoffs between performance and training effort, the overall quality of the work and its potential impact warrant a high score.

### KernelDNA: Dynamic Kernel Sharing via Decoupled Naive Adapters
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23379v1)
- **Authors**: Haiduo Huang, Yadong Zhang, Pengju Ren
- **Abstract**: Dynamic convolution enhances model capacity by adaptively combining multiple kernels, yet faces critical trade-offs: prior works either (1) incur significant parameter overhead by scaling kernel numbers linearly, (2) compromise inference speed through complex kernel interactions, or (3) struggle to jointly optimize dynamic attention and static kernels. We also observe that pre-trained Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) exhibit inter-layer redundancy akin to that in Large Language Models (LLMs). Specifically, dense convolutional layers can be efficiently replaced by derived ``child" layers generated from a shared ``parent" convolutional kernel through an adapter. To address these limitations and implement the weight-sharing mechanism, we propose a lightweight convolution kernel plug-in, named KernelDNA. It decouples kernel adaptation into input-dependent dynamic routing and pre-trained static modulation, ensuring both parameter efficiency and hardware-friendly inference. Unlike existing dynamic convolutions that expand parameters via multi-kernel ensembles, our method leverages cross-layer weight sharing and adapter-based modulation, enabling dynamic kernel specialization without altering the standard convolution structure. This design preserves the native computational efficiency of standard convolutions while enhancing representation power through input-adaptive kernel adjustments. Experiments on image classification and dense prediction tasks demonstrate that KernelDNA achieves state-of-the-art accuracy-efficiency balance among dynamic convolution variants. Our codes are available at https://github.com/haiduo/KernelDNA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces KernelDNA, a novel dynamic convolution technique designed to enhance the representation power of CNNs while mitigating the parameter overhead and inference speed issues associated with existing dynamic convolution methods. KernelDNA achieves this by decoupling kernel adaptation into input-dependent dynamic routing and pre-trained static modulation. This allows for cross-layer weight sharing, enabling the generation of derived "child" layers from a shared "parent" kernel via lightweight adapters.  By replacing dense convolutional layers with these adapted layers, the method aims to reduce parameters and maintain or improve performance. The paper presents experimental results on image classification and dense prediction tasks, demonstrating state-of-the-art accuracy-efficiency balance compared to other dynamic convolution variants.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its integration of weight-sharing, inspired by large language model compression techniques, with dynamic convolution.  The decoupling of dynamic routing and static modulation, implemented through the lightweight adapter design, appears to be a novel approach to achieving both parameter efficiency and hardware-friendly inference in dynamic convolution. Compared to existing kernel bank/generator methods, the explicit weight sharing combined with the decoupled attention mechanisms seems to be a novel approach.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a critical trade-off in dynamic convolution: the increase in model size and computational cost due to the expansion of kernels. KernelDNA's approach, by enabling dynamic kernel specialization *without* significantly altering the standard convolution structure, has the potential to make dynamic convolution more practical for real-world deployments, particularly on resource-constrained devices. The Pareto frontier analysis showing improved accuracy and efficiency trade-offs is a strong argument for the practical significance of the work. The demonstration on real-world tasks like object detection and segmentation is beneficial.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Parameter Efficiency:** The primary strength is the significant parameter reduction achieved compared to other dynamic convolution methods, particularly ODConv and CondConv, without significantly sacrificing performance.
    *   **Hardware Friendliness:** The paper emphasizes maintaining the native computational efficiency of standard convolutions, leading to inference speeds closer to static CNNs than existing dynamic alternatives. This is supported by experimental evidence (fps and latency).
    *   **Weight Sharing:** The concept of weight sharing, inspired by LLMs, is a compelling approach for reducing redundancy in CNNs.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides experiments across several architectures (ResNet, MobileNetV2, ConvNeXt), datasets (ImageNet-1K, MS-COCO), and tasks (classification, object detection, segmentation), increasing confidence in the method's generality.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Ablation studies on the adapter components and kernel sharing schemes add depth to the analysis.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost (FLOPs):** Although the paper touts hardware-friendliness, the presented FLOPs (Floating Point Operations) are not always lower compared to a static network.
    *   **Complexity:** While the modular adapter design is intended to be lightweight, the description of the channel/spatial/filter attention mechanisms can be intricate. A clearer and more intuitive explanation of the adapter's internal workings would enhance understanding.
    *   **Dependency on Static Kernels:** The method relies on an initial "parent" kernel, effectively modulating pre-trained kernels with adapters. This contrasts with methods that dynamically generate kernels *de novo*, and the influence from the 'parent' architecture is significant, limiting architectural freedom.
    *   **Limited novelty:** KernelDNA is essentially combining ideas, which have been proposed in the past.
    *   **Performance claims:** While KDNA improves performance and efficiency, the gains, while present, are not particularly transformative.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research by encouraging the integration of weight-sharing principles into dynamic convolution techniques and by highlighting the importance of hardware-friendly designs. The decoupled adaptation strategy may inspire new ways to optimize dynamic networks.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution with a clear, well-motivated approach to addressing a significant problem in dynamic convolution. The combination of weight sharing and decoupled attention leads to an accuracy-efficiency trade-off that surpasses existing methods. The experimental validation is thorough, supporting the claims made. While the technique doesn't represent a revolutionary shift in the field and is still relying on some pre-trained kernels, it has potential to be refined and adopted by others.

### ToRL: Scaling Tool-Integrated RL
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23383v1)
- **Authors**: Xuefeng Li, Haoyang Zou, Pengfei Liu
- **Abstract**: We introduce ToRL (Tool-Integrated Reinforcement Learning), a framework for training large language models (LLMs) to autonomously use computational tools via reinforcement learning. Unlike supervised fine-tuning, ToRL allows models to explore and discover optimal strategies for tool use. Experiments with Qwen2.5-Math models show significant improvements: ToRL-7B reaches 43.3\% accuracy on AIME~24, surpassing reinforcement learning without tool integration by 14\% and the best existing Tool-Integrated Reasoning (TIR) model by 17\%. Further analysis reveals emergent behaviors such as strategic tool invocation, self-regulation of ineffective code, and dynamic adaptation between computational and analytical reasoning, all arising purely through reward-driven learning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "TORL: Scaling Tool-Integrated RL":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TORL (Tool-Integrated Reinforcement Learning), a framework designed to train large language models (LLMs) to autonomously use computational tools through reinforcement learning (RL) *directly* from pre-trained base models.  Unlike methods relying on supervised fine-tuning (SFT), TORL allows the model to discover optimal tool utilization strategies through unrestricted exploration during RL.  Experiments with Qwen2.5-Math models (1.5B and 7B parameters) demonstrate significant performance improvements on mathematical problem-solving tasks (AIME). The analysis reveals emergent cognitive behaviors like strategic tool invocation, self-regulation of code generation, and dynamic switching between computational and analytical reasoning.  The authors release their implementation, datasets, and models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The primary novelty lies in the *direct* application of RL to base LLMs for tool integration *without* initial SFT.  Existing tool-augmented LLM approaches mostly rely on SFT to teach the model how and when to use tools. TORL enables the RL agent to explore the tool-augmented action space to discover better strategies.  This eliminates the reliance on human-designed (or distilled) tool-use patterns.  The observed emergent behaviors like self-regulation of code generation support the effectiveness of learning directly from base models. However, RL from scratch has also been used in similar contexts. The actual novel contributions are the specific combination with tool usage from a base model without any prior fine-tuning of any kind.

* **Significance:** The significance of the work is threefold:
    1.  **Performance Improvement:** Achieves higher accuracy on a challenging mathematical benchmark (AIME) compared to SFT-based tool-integrated reasoning and even compared to standard RL methods without tool integration.
    2.  **Emergent Behaviors:**  Observing the emergence of cognitive behaviors (strategic tool use, self-correction) through RL is significant. This suggests that RL can potentially unlock more sophisticated reasoning capabilities in LLMs than simply mimicking expert tool usage through supervised learning.
    3.  **Opens New Research Directions:**  The paper highlights trade-offs between performance, training speed, and reward design in tool-integrated RL. It provides a reproducible framework and analysis to the research community.
* **Strengths:**
    * The experimental results demonstrate clear improvements over existing techniques.
    * The analysis of emergent cognitive behaviors provides valuable insights into the workings of tool-integrated RL.
    * The open-sourcing of code, data, and models promotes reproducibility and further research.
    * The paper is well-written and structured.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Computational Cost:** Scaling RL, especially with complex action spaces like tool use, is computationally expensive.  The paper acknowledges the GPU idle time introduced during tool calls and the trade-off between tool usage frequency and training speed.  Further optimizing the RL framework for efficiency would be beneficial.
    * **Reward Design:**  While the paper implements a rule-based reward function, there is room for exploration in more sophisticated reward structures to encourage desired tool usage patterns and mitigate potential negative side effects.
    * **Limited Scope:**  The experiments are primarily focused on mathematical problem-solving.  It would be interesting to evaluate the framework on a broader range of tasks requiring different types of tools.
    * **Lack of ablations to pinpoint crucial elements:** There is no direct ablations that compares TORL against RL from SFT-trained models, which is an important baseline given the focus on unconstrained exploration.

* **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more capable and autonomous LLMs by promoting a shift from supervised imitation of tool use to reinforcement learning-driven discovery of optimal strategies.  It also provides a valuable framework and analysis for future research in this area.

**Justification of Score:**

While the work is significant and provides valuable insights, it's not groundbreaking in its general approach (RL training with LLMs). The real contribution lies in the specific combination of RL training for base models without any SFT pre-training and the exploration of emergent behaviors in the context of tool usage. The emergent behaviors show that the model is truly learning tool use strategies beyond pure imitation. The main weakness is the restricted scope with mathematical problem-solving.

### Scaling Auditory Cognition via Test-Time Compute in Audio Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23395v1)
- **Authors**: Ting Dang, Yan Gao, Hong Jia
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown exceptional versatility in natural language processing, prompting recent efforts to extend their multimodal capabilities to speech processing through the development of audio large language models (Audio LLMs). While Audio LLMs excel in tasks such as speech recognition and synthesis, it remains unclear how they perform when faced with the auditory cognitive challenges posed by real-world environments, such as audio comprehension and listening recall, particularly in the presence of background noise or overlapping speech. Unlike text-based LLMs, which have access to vast amounts of text data for pre-training, retraining Audio LLMs with diverse auditory cognitive scenes is difficult due to the limited datasets that simulate real-world auditory cognitive scenarios and the challenge of acquiring auditory cognitive labels for training. While test-time compute (TTC) methods have been shown to enhance the capabilities of text-based LLMs during inference, a key challenge lies in designing these TTC methods to improve the auditory capabilities of Audio LLMs. This study aims to address these two research gaps by: i) exploring the auditory cognitive capabilities of Audio LLMs, and ii) enhancing their capabilities using TTC approaches. We have investigated five different Audio LLMs for auditory cognition using a \textit{self-collected} database and have proposed five TTC approaches to enhance auditory cognitive capabilities during inference. Our findings reveal that Audio LLMs performance decreases in more challenging auditory cognitive tasks. The proposed TTC approaches significantly enhance cognitive auditory capabilities, advancing the development of more adaptable and resilient Audio LLMs for practical applications such as assistive listening devices, voice-based AI assistants, and communication technologies.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Scaling Auditory Cognition via Test-Time Compute in Audio Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper explores the auditory cognitive capabilities of Audio Large Language Models (LLMs) in complex, real-world listening environments and investigates how Test-Time Compute (TTC) methods can improve their performance. The authors collect a new dataset simulating real-world auditory scenarios, including background noise and overlapping speech, and then evaluate five different Audio LLMs on tasks requiring varying levels of auditory cognition, such as event recognition, recall, and computation of spoken digits. They also propose and evaluate five different TTC strategies, including Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting and various search-against-verifiers methods, to enhance the LLMs' performance during inference. The results indicate that Audio LLMs generally underperform compared to human perception, especially in challenging auditory environments, but that TTC methods can significantly improve their performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The paper tackles a relevant problem by investigating the auditory cognitive abilities of Audio LLMs in realistic settings rather than relying solely on curated, clean datasets. This is an important step towards deploying these models in real-world applications. The exploration of TTC methods to enhance auditory cognition in Audio LLMs is also a relatively unexplored area.
    *   **Relevance:** The research is highly relevant as Audio LLMs are rapidly developing, and understanding their limitations in complex auditory environments is crucial for their practical application in areas like assistive listening devices and voice assistants.
    *   **Methodology:** The study introduces a newly designed dataset simulating real-world audio environments with increasing difficulty, which directly addresses the gaps in existing data for training and evaluating audio LLMs. The exploration of multiple TTC strategies and a comparison between models and human performance offers a comprehensive perspective.
    *   **Results:** The findings provide valuable insights into the weaknesses of current Audio LLMs and the potential of TTC methods to improve their robustness in challenging environments. The performance of the various TTC strategies yields insights for future research directions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Size and Diversity:** While the creation of a custom dataset is a strength, the paper mentions only 10 participants during data collection. The dataset may be limited in size and diversity compared to existing datasets used for pre-training text-based LLMs. A larger and more diverse dataset would enhance the generalizability of the results. The demographics of the participants should also be considered when interpreting the results.
    *   **Limited Human Baseline:** Only ten individuals were included for human performance. A larger set would improve the statistical significance.
    *   **Limited Exploration of CoT Prompting:** While CoT prompting is explored, the paper mentions that the CoT prompt requires careful design. A deeper investigation into optimal CoT prompting strategies for auditory tasks could be beneficial.
    *   **Task Complexity:** The selected auditory tasks are relatively simple. While they provide a good starting point, exploring more complex auditory cognitive tasks closer to real-world applications would strengthen the paper.
    *   **Limited Generalization to Other LLMs:** The selection of LLMs in the study might not be representative of all existing models, thus the applicability of the TTC techniques to other models might vary.
    *   **LLM Verifier Choice:** The selection of GPT-4o as the LLM verifier might create a bias, as it is likely the best model of the selected group. Although that’s stated as a reason to choose it as a verifier, the impact on the evaluation could be further explored.

*   **Significance:** The paper contributes to the understanding of Audio LLMs' capabilities and limitations in real-world auditory settings and opens avenues for future research on enhancing their robustness and cognitive abilities using TTC methods. The insights gained from the experiments can inform the development of more adaptable and reliable Audio LLMs for various practical applications.

*   **Novelty Argument:**
    The novelty lies in: (1) the exploration of real-world audio cognitive abilities of LLMs, showing the performance gap to human performance; (2) proposing and experimenting with TTC methods to increase LLM performance in the specific audio domain. While TTC is not new *per se*, this paper explores a new application area. The authors also self-collect a specific dataset, which allows them to experiment on more realistic scenarios.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for Score:**
    The paper introduces novel elements in a relevant and rapidly evolving field. While the paper has limitations in dataset size, exploration of CoT prompts, and evaluation of the LLM verifier, it still introduces a strong initial approach towards improving LLM performance in real world auditory conditions.
    *The methodological soundness, including dataset collection and evaluation protocols, are adequate, but the limited human baselines could impact the strength of the paper.*

### A Large Scale Analysis of Gender Biases in Text-to-Image Generative Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23398v1)
- **Authors**: Leander Girrbach, Stephan Alaniz, Genevieve Smith, Zeynep Akata
- **Abstract**: With the increasing use of image generation technology, understanding its social biases, including gender bias, is essential. This paper presents the first large-scale study on gender bias in text-to-image (T2I) models, focusing on everyday situations. While previous research has examined biases in occupations, we extend this analysis to gender associations in daily activities, objects, and contexts. We create a dataset of 3,217 gender-neutral prompts and generate 200 images per prompt from five leading T2I models. We automatically detect the perceived gender of people in the generated images and filter out images with no person or multiple people of different genders, leaving 2,293,295 images. To enable a broad analysis of gender bias in T2I models, we group prompts into semantically similar concepts and calculate the proportion of male- and female-gendered images for each prompt. Our analysis shows that T2I models reinforce traditional gender roles, reflect common gender stereotypes in household roles, and underrepresent women in financial related activities. Women are predominantly portrayed in care- and human-centered scenarios, and men in technical or physical labor scenarios.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a large-scale analysis of gender bias in text-to-image (T2I) generative models, focusing on everyday situations. The authors create a dataset of 3,217 gender-neutral prompts across activities, contexts, objects, and occupations, generating 200 images per prompt using five state-of-the-art T2I models. They filter the images to remove unsuitable ones and then use an MLLM to automatically detect the perceived gender of people in the generated images. The analysis reveals that T2I models reinforce traditional gender roles, reflect common gender stereotypes (especially in household roles), and underrepresent women in financial activities. The study also finds that women are predominantly portrayed in care- and human-centered scenarios, while men are more often depicted in technical or physical labor scenarios.  The paper concludes that models are more likely to generate images of men by default and that, when generating images of women, it's often in stereotypical roles.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's strength lies in its scale. While previous studies have explored gender bias in T2I models, most have focused on a limited number of prompts and images, or have studied the problem only concerning occupations. This work expands the scope of the analysis to everyday activities, objects, and contexts, providing a more comprehensive picture of how gender bias manifests in T2I generation. The creation of a large dataset designed for this specific purpose is also a significant contribution. However, some aspects, particularly the *method* of bias detection, are not entirely novel. Using LLMs for gender classification of generated images has been explored before.

*   **Significance:** The findings are significant because they highlight the potential for T2I models to perpetuate and amplify existing societal biases. Given the increasing use of synthetic images in various applications (advertisements, training data), understanding and addressing these biases is crucial. The paper's emphasis on everyday situations is also vital, as these subtle biases can have a cumulative effect on reinforcing stereotypes. The clear, systematic methodology provides a template for future research in this area and offers insights that can inform the development of debiasing techniques.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Large-Scale Dataset:** The creation and use of a large, custom-built dataset contribute to the rigor and representativeness of the findings.
    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** Extending the analysis beyond occupations to everyday activities, contexts, and objects provides a more nuanced understanding of the issue.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper clearly outlines the prompt generation process, image filtering techniques, and gender identification methods, making the study reproducible.
    *   **Systematic Analysis:**  The grouping of prompts into semantic categories and the calculation of gender proportions allows for a systematic comparison across different concepts.
    *   **Comparison to Human Biases:** The authors relate the observed biases in T2I models to known human gender biases, providing a useful point of comparison.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Binary Gender Focus:** The reliance on binary gender classification ("male" or "female") is a limitation. While the authors acknowledge this, it means that the analysis does not capture the experiences of non-binary individuals or the potential for T2I models to misrepresent gender identity.
    *   **Automatic Analysis Reliance:** Although the scale necessitates automated analysis, there is always a risk that the automated methods (gender detection using MLLMs) introduce their own biases. The validation of the MLLM on a specific dataset partially mitigates this.
    *   **Lack of Model Debiasing Efforts:** The study is primarily an analysis of the biases, but the lack of even a basic attempt to debias is a missed opportunity.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development and deployment of T2I models. The dataset and code released by the authors can serve as a resource for future research. The findings can also inform the design of debiasing strategies and encourage developers to be more mindful of the potential for their models to perpetuate harmful stereotypes.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper makes a solid contribution. The *scale* and *breadth* of the analysis are noteworthy and address gaps in the existing literature. However, the use of existing techniques and lack of debiasing reduces its influence on the field.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Diffusion Meets Few-shot Class Incremental Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23402v1)
- **Authors**: Junsu Kim, Yunhoe Ku, Dongyoon Han, Seungryul Baek
- **Abstract**: Few-shot class-incremental learning (FSCIL) is challenging due to extremely limited training data; while aiming to reduce catastrophic forgetting and learn new information. We propose Diffusion-FSCIL, a novel approach that employs a text-to-image diffusion model as a frozen backbone. Our conjecture is that FSCIL can be tackled using a large generative model's capabilities benefiting from 1) generation ability via large-scale pre-training; 2) multi-scale representation; 3) representational flexibility through the text encoder. To maximize the representation capability, we propose to extract multiple complementary diffusion features to play roles as latent replay with slight support from feature distillation for preventing generative biases. Our framework realizes efficiency through 1) using a frozen backbone; 2) minimal trainable components; 3) batch processing of multiple feature extractions. Extensive experiments on CUB-200, miniImageNet, and CIFAR-100 show that Diffusion-FSCIL surpasses state-of-the-art methods, preserving performance on previously learned classes and adapting effectively to new ones.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Diffusion-FSCIL, a novel approach to few-shot class-incremental learning (FSCIL) that leverages a text-to-image diffusion model (Stable Diffusion) as a frozen backbone.  The core idea is that the pre-trained generative model's capabilities (generation, multi-scale representation, text-encoder flexibility) can be exploited for FSCIL.  The method extracts multiple complementary diffusion features through inversion and generation processes, uses latent replay with feature distillation to prevent generative biases, and learns novel class prototypes using textual inversion techniques.  Experiments on standard FSCIL benchmarks (CUB-200, miniImageNet, CIFAR-100) demonstrate superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods. The authors emphasize the efficiency of their approach through the use of a frozen backbone, minimal trainable components, and batch processing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the *effective* utilization of a text-to-image diffusion model as a backbone for FSCIL. While diffusion models have been used for other incremental learning tasks (and even some class-incremental ones), they haven't been convincingly demonstrated for *few-shot* class incremental learning.  The paper's claim of large pre-trained generative model's capabilities, while conceptually intuitive, is backed by a specific architectural implementation that extracts and combines diffusion features from both the forward and backward processes. The use of class-specific optimized prompts, drawing from textual inversion, to mitigate bias and improve generative replay is also a noteworthy contribution. The method is not merely applying a diffusion model; it's a well-engineered integration.
* **Significance:** FSCIL is a challenging and relevant problem. This paper's potential significance stems from demonstrating that a generative model can outperform discriminative models in this setting. It challenges the conventional wisdom of classification, where discriminative models are the standard choice. If the results hold up and are generalizable, it could influence future research on FSCIL. The focus on efficiency (frozen backbone, minimal training) is also important for practical applications.  The achieved state-of-the-art results on standard benchmarks certainly adds to the significance. The consistent feature extraction approach across sessions is also potentially significant, and contrasts with the way these techniques were previously used.
* **Strengths:**
    * State-of-the-art results on established benchmarks.
    * A well-designed architecture for feature extraction from the diffusion model.
    * A focus on efficiency, making the method practical.
    * Clear motivation and explanation of the approach. The paper has good abalation results that reinforce key claims.
    * Code availability enhances reproducibility and further research.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * The paper leans heavily on the architecture and pre-trained knowledge of Stable Diffusion.  Generalizability to other types of generative models (e.g., GANs, VAEs) or different diffusion architectures needs to be addressed in future research.
    * While ablation studies are included, a deeper exploration of the hyperparameter sensitivity (especially of the distillation loss weighting) would strengthen the results.
    * The "optimized prompt" strategy, while effective, adds complexity and another training stage. This could be a potential bottleneck.  Further investigation into alternative prompt strategies that require less training could be valuable.
    * The improvement on base session performance compared to Yourself, while not as strong initially, hints that the method initially requires more data to realize benefits.

* **Impact:** The paper has a very good chance of influencing future research in FSCIL. It convincingly demonstrates the power of large, pre-trained generative models in overcoming limitations of the existing discriminative model-based methods. The proposed Diffusion-FSCIL architecture is a reasonable baseline for future research to improve on. The optimized prompt learning method could be utilized in other FSCIL approaches.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-executed approach to a challenging problem, demonstrating impressive results and offering a novel perspective using a diffusion model backbone. While the approach is heavily reliant on Stable Diffusion and the optimized prompt strategy, the effective integration, the state-of-the-art performance, and the potential for future research merit a high score. The weaknesses are primarily about the need for further exploration and generalizability, rather than fundamental flaws.

### An Analysis of Decoding Methods for LLM-based Agents for Faithful Multi-Hop Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23415v1)
- **Authors**: Alexander Murphy, Mohd Sanad Zaki Rizvi, Aden Haussmann, Ping Nie, Guifu Liu, Aryo Pradipta Gema, Pasquale Minervini
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) frequently produce factually inaccurate outputs - a phenomenon known as hallucination - which limits their accuracy in knowledge-intensive NLP tasks. Retrieval-augmented generation and agentic frameworks such as Reasoning and Acting (ReAct) can address this issue by giving the model access to external knowledge. However, LLMs often fail to remain faithful to retrieved information. Mitigating this is critical, especially if LLMs are required to reason about the retrieved information. Recent research has explored training-free decoding strategies to improve the faithfulness of model generations. We present a systematic analysis of how the combination of the ReAct framework and decoding strategies (i.e., DeCoRe, DoLa, and CAD) can influence the faithfulness of LLM-generated answers. Our results show that combining an agentic framework for knowledge retrieval with decoding methods that enhance faithfulness can increase accuracy on the downstream Multi-Hop Question Answering tasks. For example, we observe an F1 increase from 19.5 to 32.6 on HotpotQA when using ReAct and DoLa.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates how different decoding methods influence the faithfulness of Large Language Model (LLM)-based agents, particularly within the Reasoning and Acting (ReAct) framework, for multi-hop question answering. The authors systematically analyze the impact of combining ReAct with training-free decoding strategies like DeCoRe, DoLa, and CAD on the factual accuracy of LLM-generated answers. The study evaluates these combinations on three multi-hop question-answering datasets (HotpotQA, 2WikiMultihopQA, MuSiQue) using Qwen2-7b-Instruct and Llama3-8b-Instruct.  The results demonstrate that faithful decoding methods, when integrated with ReAct, can enhance accuracy and faithfulness in the answers, as well as improving adherance to the ReAct framework's prescribed output format. The paper also highlights the potential limitations of faithful decoding when relevant context is absent or unretrievable.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Systematic Analysis:** The paper offers a well-structured and systematic analysis of the interplay between agentic frameworks like ReAct and different decoding strategies for improving faithfulness in LLM-based question answering. This is valuable as it moves beyond simply applying ReAct and delves into *how* we can improve its knowledge-intensive capabilities.
    *   **Practical Approach:** The focus on training-free decoding strategies makes the proposed methods practically applicable without requiring extensive fine-tuning. This lowers the entry barrier for researchers and practitioners.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The empirical results across multiple datasets and models provide strong evidence supporting the effectiveness of combining ReAct with faithful decoding techniques for downstream question answering.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** The inclusion of a qualitative analysis helps illustrate the reasoning differences between standard and context-aware decoding methods, providing valuable insights into *why* faithful decoders lead to better outcomes.
    *   **Format Adherance Improvement:** Demonstrating improved prompt format adherence when using faithful decoders is a significant finding.  It suggests these decoders can make the ReAct framework more reliable, reducing the need for extensive prompt engineering or fine-tuning for specific output formats.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Decoder Scope:** While the paper examines several decoding methods, the field is rapidly evolving. The analysis could be further strengthened by including newer or more advanced decoding approaches.
    *   **Context Dependency:** The paper acknowledges that the benefits of faithful decoding are contingent on the availability of relevant context. Exploring methods to improve context retrieval in conjunction with decoding strategies could provide a more complete solution.  This could include analyzing scenarios where faithful decoding *hurts* performance due to incorrect or misleading retrieved context.
    *   **Model Specificity:** The experiments primarily use Qwen2-7b-Instruct and Llama3-8b-Instruct.  A broader range of model sizes and architectures would further validate the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Lack of Deep Dive into Error Cases:** While a qualitative analysis is included, the paper could benefit from a more thorough investigation of specific error cases. This could help identify the types of errors that are mitigated by the decoding strategies, as well as the types of errors that still persist.
    *   **Limited Insight into Decoder Interaction:** The study lacks detailed explanations on *why* certain decoders work better with ReAct than others. Addressing this point would greatly enhance the paper's theoretical contribution.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The paper makes a valuable contribution by systematically analyzing the compatibility of ReAct with faithful decoding methods. Demonstrating that faithful decoding strategies increase accuracy, retrieval performance, and adherence to reasoning formats constitutes a substantial advance. The focus on training-free approaches further enhances its practical relevance. However, the study also acknowledges limitations, such as the dependence on accurate knowledge retrieval, which limits the potential significance. While not groundbreaking, this work represents a solid step toward enhancing the reliability of LLM-based agents.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a valuable, systematic exploration of a practical problem (improving faithfulness in LLM agents) with verifiable results. However, the limitations mentioned above—scope of decoders, model specificity, context dependence, and limited analysis of error cases and decoder interaction—prevent a higher score.

### A Multi-agent Onboarding Assistant based on Large Language Models, Retrieval Augmented Generation, and Chain-of-Thought
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23421v1)
- **Authors**: Andrei Cristian Ionescu, Sergey Titov, Maliheh Izadi
- **Abstract**: Effective onboarding in software engineering is crucial but difficult due to the fast-paced evolution of technologies. Traditional methods, like exploration and workshops, are costly, time-consuming, and quickly outdated in large projects. We propose the Onboarding Buddy system, which leverages large language models, retrieval augmented generation, and an automated chain-of-thought approach to improve onboarding. It integrates dynamic, context-specific support within the development environment, offering natural language explanations, code insights, and project guidance. Our solution is agent-based and provides customized assistance with minimal human intervention. Our study results among the eight participants show an average helpfulness rating of (M=3.26, SD=0.86) and ease of onboarding at (M=3.0, SD=0.96) out of four. While similar to tools like GitHub Copilot, Onboarding Buddy uniquely integrates a chain-of-thought reasoning mechanism with retrieval-augmented generation, tailored specifically for dynamic onboarding contexts. While our initial evaluation is based on eight participants within one project, we will explore larger teams and multiple real-world codebases in the company to demonstrate broader applicability. Overall, Onboarding Buddy holds great potential for enhancing developer productivity and satisfaction. Our tool, source code, and demonstration video are publicly available
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Onboarding Buddy," an AI-powered onboarding assistant designed to streamline the process of integrating new developers into software projects.  The system utilizes large language models (LLMs), retrieval augmented generation (RAG), and chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning to provide contextualized support, including code explanations, project guidance, and natural language answers to developer queries. Onboarding Buddy is agent-based, aiming for minimal human intervention and personalized assistance. The authors evaluated their tool with eight participants performing onboarding tasks on a closed-source project. The results indicate a positive reception, with high ratings for helpfulness and ease of onboarding.  The paper also details the system's architecture, focusing on the agent-centric design, the use of a planning scratchpad for CoT reasoning, and specialized agents for contextualization, step processing, and message enhancement.  The authors highlight the tool's potential to reduce mentor burden and improve developer productivity and satisfaction.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the integration of existing technologies (LLMs, RAG, CoT) specifically for the domain of software engineering onboarding. While the individual components are not novel, the combination and tailoring to onboarding present a potentially valuable contribution. The claim that Onboarding Buddy tackles the unique challenges of onboarding, which are not covered by the general coding assistants such as GitHub Copilot, adds to its relative novelty. The agent-centric design and the "scratchpad" for tracking reasoning steps are also claimed as distinguishing features.

* **Significance:** The problem of effective onboarding is genuinely important in software engineering. Reducing mentor burden, speeding up developer integration, and improving developer satisfaction are all highly desirable outcomes. The potential impact of such a tool is thus significant. The study results, albeit with a small sample size, lend credence to the claim that the tool improves helpfulness and ease of onboarding. If the tool is scalable and can generalize to other codebases and teams, it could have a meaningful impact. However, more extensive experiments would be needed to confirm the scalability and generalizability of the approach.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of software onboarding.
    * **Well-Defined Solution:** The Onboarding Buddy system is described in detail, with a clear explanation of its architecture and components.
    * **Empirical Evaluation:**  Although small, the user study provides initial evidence supporting the tool's effectiveness.
    * **Integration of Multiple Techniques:** The system demonstrates effective integration of various techniques (LLMs, RAG, CoT) for a specific purpose.
    * **Emphasis on Context:** The tool is designed to offer context-specific assistance, moving beyond generic code suggestions.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Small Sample Size:** The evaluation is based on only eight participants within a single closed-source project.  This limits the generalizability of the findings.
    * **Limited Scope of Tasks:** The onboarding tasks were relatively basic (environment setup, adding a payment option, duplicating a questionnaire). These tasks may not fully capture the complexities of onboarding in real-world scenarios.
    * **Lack of Comparison to Baselines:** The paper does not directly compare Onboarding Buddy to existing onboarding tools (e.g., GitHub Copilot, ChatGPT Plugins) or standard onboarding practices, making it difficult to assess its relative effectiveness.
    * **Closed-Source Project:** Although the choice of a closed-source project is justifiable to avoid data contamination, it also makes it harder to independently verify the findings and assess the tool's performance on diverse codebases.
    * **Claims of being more 'dynamic'** are not clearly supported by explanations of specific mechanisms that dynamically adjust to unique onboarding needs.

* **Potential Influence:**
If Onboarding Buddy can be shown to scale to larger teams, diverse projects, and broader onboarding tasks, it has the potential to be a valuable tool for software companies. The modular design and the use of a planning scratchpad could inspire other researchers to explore similar approaches for automating and improving software engineering workflows. The research could also encourage the development of more context-aware and personalized coding assistants.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the paper's novelty in integrating existing techniques for a specific and important problem, combined with the initial positive user feedback and its architectural clarity, I'd rate it a 6. The small sample size, limited task scope, and lack of comparison to baseline methods limit the strength of the evidence and the overall significance of the contribution. The paper presents a promising approach with clear directions for future research. A score higher than 6 would require more robust experimental evidence and a more compelling demonstration of the tool's advantages over existing approaches.

### CoRanking: Collaborative Ranking with Small and Large Ranking Agents
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23427v2)
- **Authors**: Wenhan Liu, Xinyu Ma, Yutao Zhu, Lixin Su, Shuaiqiang Wang, Dawei Yin, Zhicheng Dou
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated superior listwise ranking performance. However, their superior performance often relies on large-scale parameters (\eg, GPT-4) and a repetitive sliding window process, which introduces significant efficiency challenges. In this paper, we propose \textbf{CoRanking}, a novel collaborative ranking framework that combines small and large ranking models for efficient and effective ranking. CoRanking first employs a small-size reranker to pre-rank all the candidate passages, bringing relevant ones to the top part of the list (\eg, top-20). Then, the LLM listwise reranker is applied to only rerank these top-ranked passages instead of the whole list, substantially enhancing overall ranking efficiency. Although more efficient, previous studies have revealed that the LLM listwise reranker have significant positional biases on the order of input passages. Directly feed the top-ranked passages from small reranker may result in the sub-optimal performance of LLM listwise reranker. To alleviate this problem, we introduce a passage order adjuster trained via reinforcement learning, which reorders the top passages from the small reranker to align with the LLM's preferences of passage order. Extensive experiments on three IR benchmarks demonstrate that CoRanking significantly improves efficiency (reducing ranking latency by about 70\%) while achieving even better effectiveness compared to using only the LLM listwise reranker.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CoRanking, a collaborative ranking framework designed to improve the efficiency and effectiveness of large language model (LLM) based ranking systems. Recognizing the computational cost and latency associated with applying large LLMs to re-rank entire lists of candidate passages, CoRanking proposes a multi-stage approach. First, a smaller, more efficient reranker pre-ranks the candidate passages.  Then, a passage order adjuster, trained using reinforcement learning (specifically DPO), reorders the top-ranked passages from the smaller reranker to better align with the LLM's inherent positional biases. Finally, the LLM listwise reranker is applied only to this smaller set of reordered passages. The paper presents experimental results on several IR benchmarks, demonstrating that CoRanking significantly reduces ranking latency while achieving comparable or even superior effectiveness compared to using the LLM listwise reranker alone.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The combination of small and large ranking agents isn't completely novel. Hybrid approaches to information retrieval are well-established. The core novelty lies in the specific combination of components and the integration of a passage order adjuster trained with reinforcement learning (DPO) to address the positional biases inherent in LLMs. The S³ strategy for creating high-quality DPO training data is also a novel contribution. Addressing positional bias is not new in the context of search, but addressing it specifically within an LLM reranking pipeline via a learned order adjustment layer is a meaningful contribution.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical practical challenge in deploying LLMs for ranking: their computational cost.  By demonstrating a substantial reduction in latency without sacrificing effectiveness, the research provides a valuable contribution towards making LLM-based ranking more feasible for real-world applications. The gains in efficiency are substantial (around 70% latency reduction). The results also show improved effectiveness, indicating that the passage order adjuster is not just a cost-saving measure, but actively improves the ranking quality by mitigating the impact of positional bias. The results across multiple datasets strengthen the impact. The generalizability of the findings to different LLMs is explored in the experiments, adding further significance.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of using LLMs for ranking.
    * **Well-Defined Solution:** CoRanking and its components are clearly described.
    * **Thorough Evaluation:** The experiments are extensive, covering several datasets and comparing against strong baselines. Ablation studies validate the contribution of each component.
    * **Addressing a Practical Issue:**  The research addresses a real-world constraint (computational cost) that hinders the adoption of LLM-based ranking.
    * **Generalizability Evidence**: The experiments investigate the generalizability of the approach with different LLMs.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** The CoRanking framework introduces additional complexity to the ranking pipeline, requiring the training and maintenance of multiple models. This could increase the operational overhead.
    * **Dependency on Smaller Reranker**: Performance still relies on the smaller reranker to elevate relevant documents to the top. If this component fails, CoRanking's overall accuracy is affected.
    * **Limited Exploration of Alternative Bias Mitigation**: While the reinforcement learning based order adjustment is novel, there's limited discussion or evaluation of alternative, simpler techniques for mitigating positional bias.
    * **The choice of 72B model might be prohibitive for certain applications**.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence the field by providing a practical and effective solution for deploying LLMs in ranking systems. The idea of using a smaller reranker in combination with a passage order adjuster could inspire further research on efficient and effective LLM-based ranking methods. Future work will likely explore the use of different reinforcement learning algorithms, different architectures for the passage order adjuster, and techniques for further reducing computational costs.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a significant problem in LLM-based ranking, with strong empirical evidence to support its claims. While the increased complexity of the framework and dependence on the initial reranker are valid concerns, the substantial gains in efficiency and effectiveness justify a high score. The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by providing a practical solution that addresses the computational cost of LLMs for ranking. The work is well-motivated, clearly presented, and thoroughly evaluated. The exploration of generalizability to different LLMs strengthens the significance of the findings. While the novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary, the combination of elements and the practical impact warrant a score of 8.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Speculative End-Turn Detector for Efficient Speech Chatbot Assistant
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23439v1)
- **Authors**: Hyunjong Ok, Suho Yoo, Jaeho Lee
- **Abstract**: Spoken dialogue systems powered by large language models have demonstrated remarkable abilities in understanding human speech and generating appropriate spoken responses. However, these systems struggle with end-turn detection (ETD) -- the ability to distinguish between user turn completion and hesitation. This limitation often leads to premature or delayed responses, disrupting the flow of spoken conversations. In this paper, we introduce the ETD Dataset, the first public dataset for end-turn detection. The ETD dataset consists of both synthetic speech data generated with text-to-speech models and real-world speech data collected from web sources. We also propose SpeculativeETD, a novel collaborative inference framework that balances efficiency and accuracy to improve real-time ETD in resource-constrained environments. Our approach jointly employs a lightweight GRU-based model, which rapidly detects the non-speaking units in real-time on local devices, and a high-performance Wav2vec-based model running on the server to make a more challenging classification of distinguishing turn ends from mere pauses. Experiments demonstrate that the proposed SpeculativeETD significantly improves ETD accuracy while keeping the required computations low. Datasets and code will be available after the review.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new dataset, the ETD (End-Turn Detection) dataset, specifically designed for training and evaluating end-turn detection models in spoken dialogue systems. The ETD dataset includes both synthetic speech data generated from text dialogues using TTS models and real-world speech data collected from web sources.  Alongside the dataset, the authors propose SpeculativeETD, a collaborative inference framework to improve real-time ETD performance in resource-constrained environments. SpeculativeETD uses a lightweight GRU-based model on local devices for initial detection of non-speaking units and a high-performance Wav2vec-based model on the server for more challenging classifications of pauses versus turn ends. Experiments demonstrate that SpeculativeETD improves ETD accuracy while maintaining low computational requirements.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The creation and release of the ETD dataset are a significant contribution. The scarcity of publicly available datasets for ETD is a recognized bottleneck in the field. The combination of synthetic and real-world data addresses this issue and enables further research. The SpeculativeETD framework is a novel approach to balancing accuracy and efficiency for real-time ETD. The core idea of using a lightweight model for quick, local detection and a more powerful model on the server for complex cases is a sensible and potentially impactful design.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work lies in addressing a practical problem in spoken dialogue systems: accurately detecting when a user has finished speaking. Improved ETD directly translates to more natural and responsive conversational interactions, which is crucial for user experience. The SpeculativeETD framework offers a practical solution for deploying ETD models in resource-constrained environments like mobile devices, thus expanding the applicability of these systems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset Contribution:** The ETD dataset is a valuable resource for the community. The inclusion of both synthetic and real data is a strength, providing a balanced training and evaluation set.
    *   **Practical Approach:** SpeculativeETD focuses on a real-world problem and provides a practical solution that considers both accuracy and computational constraints.
    *   **Clear Experimental Results:** The paper provides experimental evidence to support the effectiveness of the SpeculativeETD framework, demonstrating improved accuracy compared to a lightweight model and reduced computational cost compared to a heavier model.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Real-World Data Analysis:** The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the characteristics of the real-world data and how it compares to the synthetic data. Understanding the differences and potential biases in each dataset would strengthen the work.
    *   **Baseline comparison:** The baselines, while reasonable, might not include the very latest advancements in efficient ASR or other edge-computing techniques. More recent and powerful baselines would provide a stronger benchmark.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** While standard, the evaluation focuses primarily on accuracy-based metrics. A more comprehensive evaluation could include user studies to assess the perceived quality of the conversation and the impact of improved ETD on user experience.

*   **Potential Influence:** The ETD dataset has the potential to stimulate further research in end-turn detection and encourage the development of more robust and efficient ETD models. The SpeculativeETD framework could inspire similar collaborative inference approaches for other tasks in spoken dialogue systems. The idea of combining local, lightweight processing with remote, more powerful processing is applicable in many scenarios.

**Justification:**

While the paper presents a valuable dataset and a novel framework, the improvements demonstrated are incremental, and the scope is limited. The SpeculativeETD improves the speed without significant loss in accuracy and the novelty could be debated given previous works on two-stage processing. There are weaknesses and areas to improve upon like the depth of analysis. The dataset and SpeculativeETD is relevant and addresses a practical problem, so it's important.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Semantic-Preserving Transformations as Mutation Operators: A Study on Their Effectiveness in Defect Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23448v1)
- **Authors**: Max Hort, Linas Vidziunas, Leon Moonen
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in defect detection use language models. Existing works enhanced the training data to improve the models' robustness when applied to semantically identical code (i.e., predictions should be the same). However, the use of semantically identical code has not been considered for improving the tools during their application - a concept closely related to metamorphic testing. The goal of our study is to determine whether we can use semantic-preserving transformations, analogue to mutation operators, to improve the performance of defect detection tools in the testing stage. We first collect existing publications which implemented semantic-preserving transformations and share their implementation, such that we can reuse them. We empirically study the effectiveness of three different ensemble strategies for enhancing defect detection tools. We apply the collected transformations on the Devign dataset, considering vulnerabilities as a type of defect, and two fine-tuned large language models for defect detection (VulBERTa, PLBART). We found 28 publications with 94 different transformations. We choose to implement 39 transformations from four of the publications, but a manual check revealed that 23 out 39 transformations change code semantics. Using the 16 remaining, correct transformations and three ensemble strategies, we were not able to increase the accuracy of the defect detection models. Our results show that reusing shared semantic-preserving transformation is difficult, sometimes even causing wrongful changes to the semantics. Keywords: defect detection, language model, semantic-preserving transformation, ensemble
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of semantic-preserving transformations, analogous to mutation operators, to improve the performance of defect detection tools, specifically Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors analyze existing literature, identifying semantic-preserving transformations that can be replicated. They then empirically study the effectiveness of different ensemble strategies that leverage predictions from code transformed using these transformations, with the aim of enhancing defect detection.  They use the Devign dataset (for vulnerability defects) and the VulBERTa and PLBART LLMs for evaluation.  The key finding is that while many semantic-preserving transformations are documented, reliably implementing them is difficult, with many altering code semantics. The authors' experiments ultimately failed to improve defect detection accuracy using the selected correct transformations and ensemble methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using semantic-preserving transformations *during testing* to improve LLM-based defect detection, inspired by metamorphic testing, is moderately novel. While previous work used such transformations for data augmentation during training, this paper focuses on enhancing the application/testing phase.  The rigorous effort to collect and categorize existing transformations is also a valuable contribution, although hampered by implementation difficulties.

*   **Significance:** The paper has mixed significance.  On one hand, the negative results are important because they show a direct application of a promising technique (semantic-preserving transformations) *doesn't* automatically improve defect detection, suggesting that the application is more complex than anticipated. The identification of challenges in replicating existing transformations and ensuring semantic preservation is valuable for future research.  On the other hand, the failure to achieve improved accuracy is a limitation. The impact is further limited as the experiment is only run on one dataset (Devign), using only two LLMs, although these models were chosen because of the existing implementation and the desire to avoid additional training costs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Thorough Literature Review:** The paper conducts a comprehensive review of semantic-preserving transformations in software engineering.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The research question is well-defined and motivated.
    *   **Empirical Approach:** The paper uses an empirical approach to evaluate the effectiveness of semantic-preserving transformations.
    *   **Honest Reporting of Negative Results:** The authors transparently report the challenges encountered and the failure to achieve improved accuracy.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:**  The paper provides a detailed analysis of the transformations, their applicability, and their impact on predictions.
    *   **Artifact Sharing:** The sharing of transformations and results for replicability is a commendable practice.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** The study is limited to a single dataset and two LLMs.
    *   **Implementation Difficulties:** The significant number of transformations that altered semantics highlights the challenges in reusing existing resources, but also suggests a lack of rigor in earlier publications/implementations.
    *   **Unclear Reasoning Behind Transformation Selection:** The selection criteria for the initial transformations could be more rigorous. Was any consideration made towards selecting transformations that address commonly observed failure modes in LLM defect detection or vulnerabilities known to be difficult to detect?
    *   **Ensemble Methods:** The ensemble methods explored are relatively simple. More sophisticated approaches, such as stacking with more complex meta-learners, could potentially yield better results.  It would also be useful to understand the *reasons* for failure, such as the ensemble approach not being able to capture the interaction between the different LLMs.
    *   **Lack of Statistical Significance Testing**: The paper lacks statistical testing to confidently state that the changes in accuracy, though numerically different, are not due to random chance.

*   **Potential Influence:** While the paper did not achieve its primary goal, it contributes to the body of knowledge by identifying challenges and limitations. It also calls for more rigorous development and validation of semantic-preserving transformations and may inspire more sophisticated methods for leveraging transformed code in defect detection.

**Score:** 5

**Justification:**

The paper possesses reasonable novelty in its application of metamorphic testing principles to the application phase of LLM-based defect detection. The thorough literature review and the honest reporting of results are commendable. However, the limited scope of the empirical evaluation, the encountered implementation difficulties, the relatively simple ensemble methods, and the lack of statistical testing limit the overall significance of the paper. While the negative results are valuable, the study does not provide a clear path towards improved defect detection. The challenges encountered in re-implementing transformations lower the practical impact of the work, even though these transformations are shared. Therefore, a score of 5 reflects the mixed contributions and limitations of the study.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 5/10

### AU-TTT: Vision Test-Time Training model for Facial Action Unit Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23450v1)
- **Authors**: Bohao Xing, Kaishen Yuan, Zitong Yu, Xin Liu, Heikki Kälviäinen
