- **Score**: 8/10

### Mapping Geopolitical Bias in 11 Large Language Models: A Bilingual, Dual-Framing Analysis of U.S.-China Tensions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23688v1)
- **Authors**: William Guey, Pierrick Bougault, Vitor D. de Moura, Wei Zhang, Jose O. Gomes
- **Abstract**: This study systematically analyzes geopolitical bias across 11 prominent Large Language Models (LLMs) by examining their responses to seven critical topics in U.S.-China relations. Utilizing a bilingual (English and Chinese) and dual-framing (affirmative and reverse) methodology, we generated 19,712 prompts designed to detect ideological leanings in model outputs. Responses were quantitatively assessed on a normalized scale from -2 (strongly Pro-China) to +2 (strongly Pro-U.S.) and categorized according to stance, neutrality, and refusal rates. The findings demonstrate significant and consistent ideological alignments correlated with the LLMs' geographic origins; U.S.-based models predominantly favored Pro-U.S. stances, while Chinese-origin models exhibited pronounced Pro-China biases. Notably, language and prompt framing substantially influenced model responses, with several LLMs exhibiting stance reversals based on prompt polarity or linguistic context. Additionally, we introduced comprehensive metrics to evaluate response consistency across languages and framing conditions, identifying variability and vulnerabilities in model behaviors. These results offer practical insights that can guide organizations and individuals in selecting LLMs best aligned with their operational priorities and geopolitical considerations, underscoring the importance of careful model evaluation in politically sensitive applications. Furthermore, the research highlights specific prompt structures and linguistic variations that can strategically trigger distinct responses from models, revealing methods for effectively navigating and influencing LLM outputs.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Mapping Geopolitical Bias in 11 Large Language Models: A Bilingual, Dual-Framing Analysis of U.S.–China Tensions":

**Summary:**

This paper systematically investigates geopolitical biases in 11 large language models (LLMs) regarding U.S.-China relations. The authors employ a bilingual (English and Chinese) and dual-framing (affirmative and reversed prompts) approach to generate a dataset of nearly 20,000 prompts. They quantitatively analyze model responses on a scale from Pro-China to Pro-U.S., assessing stance, neutrality, and refusal rates. The study finds that LLMs exhibit significant and consistent ideological alignments correlated with their geographic origins: U.S.-based models leaning Pro-U.S. and Chinese models leaning Pro-China.  Language and prompt framing substantially influence model responses, particularly in Chinese models, with reversals observed based on prompt polarity. The paper also introduces metrics to assess response consistency and identifies variability and vulnerabilities in model behavior. The authors conclude by discussing the implications of these findings for organizations selecting LLMs and navigating model outputs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Systematic and Rigorous Methodology:** The paper employs a highly systematic approach to bias detection. The use of bilingual prompts, dual framing (affirmative and negative phrasing), randomization, and repeated iterations considerably strengthens the study's internal validity. The quantitative analysis provides a more objective assessment of bias compared to purely qualitative approaches. The evaluation metrics (neutrality and refusal rates) add depth to the analysis.
    *   **Relevance and Significance:** The topic of geopolitical bias in LLMs is highly relevant, given their increasing influence on public discourse and decision-making, the U.S.-China relationship is obviously a salient geopolitical tension. The paper addresses a critical gap in existing research, which has largely focused on other forms of bias (gender, race).
    *   **Practical Insights:** The study provides practical insights for organizations and individuals selecting and using LLMs, including information on which models might be more suited to certain geopolitical viewpoints. The analysis of prompt sensitivity and linguistic variations could inform strategies for navigating and influencing LLM outputs. The emphasis on language and framing effects is important, providing practical ways to mitigate bias.
    *   **Clear Presentation of Results:** The paper presents its findings clearly, with visualizations (though small and hard to read, but there), supporting the analysis.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The study focuses solely on U.S.-China relations and a specific set of seven questions. The findings may not generalize to other geopolitical contexts or issue areas (such as Russia-Ukraine, for example).
    *   **Opaque Model Selection:** While the paper mentions the LLMs used (five from China, five from the US, and one European), there is little justification to *why* these LLMs where specifically chosen, and not other models.
    *   **Reliance on GPT-4o-mini:** The stance-detection relied on a *secondary* LLM for classification, which introduces another potential bias/noise into the process.
    *   **Static Snapshot:** The study is a snapshot in time. LLMs are constantly evolving, and the observed biases could change as models are updated and retrained. The rapid pace of LLM development diminishes the long-term relevance.
    *   **Lack of Causal Explanations:** The paper identifies correlations between model origin, language, framing, and bias, but it does not provide deep causal explanations. It can only speculate on the underlying mechanisms driving these biases (e.g., training data, safety filters).

    *   **Limited discussion of cultural nuance:** The paper recognizes language effects but could benefit from a deeper discussion of cultural nuances that are relevant to U.S.-China relationships and AI.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

The paper offers *some* novelty by combining several factors:

*   **Geopolitical focus:** Prior research has explored biases in LLMs, but this study specifically targets geopolitical alignments related to US-China relations.
*   **Bilingual prompts and dual framing:** This is a strong addition that improves robustness and enables the discovery of nuances that a single-language, single-framing approach might miss.
*   **Systematic methodology and quantitative analysis:** The paper adds rigor to the field by using a systematic experimental design and quantitative measurements to assess biases in LLMs.

*   **Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence:

*   **Responsible AI development:** It adds to the growing understanding of biases in AI and the need for responsible development practices.
*   **Model selection:** The findings can inform how organizations and individuals choose LLMs for geopolitical contexts.
*   **Bias mitigation strategies:** The study reveals the importance of framing and language, which are crucial considerations for developing bias mitigation strategies.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper is a well-executed, timely, and relevant study that contributes to our understanding of geopolitical biases in LLMs. While the limitations related to generalizability, static snapshots, and casual explanations are important considerations, the systematic methodology and practical insights add considerable value.

**Score: 6.5**

**Justification:** While the paper is relevant and addresses an important topic, the limitations and the narrow focus prevent it from achieving a higher score. While it’s well-executed, the inherent rapid advancements of LLMs make any fixed “snapshot” study somewhat dated relatively soon, reducing its long-term significance. The reliance on LLM’s for stance-detection also introduces a degree of circularity, and reduces the overall reliability. Nevertheless, the rigorous methodology and the focus on bilingual prompts and dual framing make it a significant step forward in analyzing political alignment in LLMs, as well as potential strategies for mitigating these biases.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### A Conceptual Framework for Human-AI Collaborative Genome Annotation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23691v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaomei Li, Alex Whan, Meredith McNeil, David Starns, Jessica Irons, Samuel C. Andrew, Rad Suchecki
- **Abstract**: Genome annotation is essential for understanding the functional elements within genomes. While automated methods are indispensable for processing large-scale genomic data, they often face challenges in accurately predicting gene structures and functions. Consequently, manual curation by domain experts remains crucial for validating and refining these predictions. These combined outcomes from automated tools and manual curation highlight the importance of integrating human expertise with AI capabilities to improve both the accuracy and efficiency of genome annotation. However, the manual curation process is inherently labor-intensive and time-consuming, making it difficult to scale for large datasets. To address these challenges, we propose a conceptual framework, Human-AI Collaborative Genome Annotation (HAICoGA), which leverages the synergistic partnership between humans and artificial intelligence to enhance human capabilities and accelerate the genome annotation process. Additionally, we explore the potential of integrating Large Language Models (LLMs) into this framework to support and augment specific tasks. Finally, we discuss emerging challenges and outline open research questions to guide further exploration in this area.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a conceptual framework called HAICOGA (Human-AI Collaborative Genome Annotation) to improve the accuracy and efficiency of genome annotation. It addresses the limitations of both automated annotation methods and manual curation by integrating human expertise and AI capabilities. The framework encompasses key elements like humans, AI systems and tools, data, goals and tasks, a human-machine interface, environment, and collaboration. The authors explore using Large Language Models (LLMs) to augment specific tasks within this framework and discuss challenges and future research directions. The paper envisions a multi-agent system where specialized AI agents collaborate with humans in various annotation tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The explicit articulation of HAICOGA as a comprehensive conceptual framework for genome annotation appears to be novel. While the individual components (automated annotation, manual curation, AI-human interaction) are not new, their structured integration into a cohesive framework with specific consideration for genome annotation is a contribution.  The paper moves beyond just saying AI can help and delves into *how* this collaboration might be structured and managed.  However, the novelty is limited by the fact that many of the components of the HAICOGA framework are already being explored individually in various research projects.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem: the bottleneck in genome annotation created by the limitations of purely automated methods and the labor-intensiveness of manual curation. By proposing a concrete framework for AI-human collaboration, it offers a potential solution for accelerating the annotation process while improving accuracy. This could lead to a deeper understanding of genomes and facilitate advancements in various fields, including medicine, agriculture, and biotechnology.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Framework:** HAICOGA provides a structured approach that systematically considers all the critical elements required for successful AI-human collaboration in genome annotation.
*   **Focus on LLMs:** The paper acknowledges the potential of LLMs in enhancing AI's capabilities in this domain, offering insights into how they can be integrated into the framework.
*   **Discussion of Challenges:**  The authors realistically discuss the limitations of current AI methods (e.g., hallucinations in LLMs) and the challenges of building such collaborative systems.
*   **Future Research Directions:** The paper identifies open research questions that can guide future exploration in this area, giving researchers a roadmap for advancement.
* **Glossary**: The inclusion of a glossary helps readers with varied experience to comprehend the specific terminology used in the field.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Lack of Concrete Implementation:** The paper remains primarily conceptual. There is no demonstration of the framework's effectiveness through a real-world implementation or case study.
*   **High-Level Description:** While the framework provides a structure, the specific details on how to design the agents, the communication protocols, and the evaluation metrics for each component are not fully fleshed out. It is more of a blueprint than a detailed engineering specification.
*   **Limited Discussion of Existing Collaborative Tools:** While acknowledging the existence of tools like Apollo, the paper doesn't fully analyze how HAICOGA differs from or builds upon these existing platforms. A clearer comparison would strengthen the framework's positioning.
*   **Potential for Overcomplexity:** Implementing HAICOGA effectively could be complex and resource-intensive, as it requires the integration of various AI tools, human expertise, and data sources. This may limit its adoption in resource-constrained settings.
*   **Limited emphasis on explainability**: Although the paper briefly mentions "explainability", it is not well addressed for multi-agent environments. Lack of explainability and trust can reduce the overall impact of collaborative frameworks.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the development of AI-assisted genome annotation tools and workflows. By providing a solid conceptual foundation, it can guide researchers and developers in designing effective and collaborative AI systems. The framework could be adopted by research institutions, biotechnology companies, and government agencies involved in genome annotation. Also, this can facilitate further research.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **6**. While the HAICOGA framework is a valuable contribution that provides a systematic approach for AI-human collaboration in genome annotation, its lack of concrete implementation and detailed design specifications limits its immediate impact. The paper acknowledges the potential of LLMs while also honestly admitting to known limitations. The discussion of challenges and future directions is a significant strength, but the paper could benefit from a more comprehensive analysis of existing collaborative tools and a clearer articulation of its unique contributions. HAICOGA has high potential to serve as a valuable stepping stone in the future development of a more comprehensive framework but, in its current form, falls slightly short of being revolutionary.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: q-bio.GN
- **Score**: 6/10

### Expanding-and-Shrinking Binary Neural Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23709v1)
- **Authors**: Xulong Shi, Caiyi Sun, Zhi Qi, Liu Hao, Xiaodong Yang
- **Abstract**: While binary neural networks (BNNs) offer significant benefits in terms of speed, memory and energy, they encounter substantial accuracy degradation in challenging tasks compared to their real-valued counterparts. Due to the binarization of weights and activations, the possible values of each entry in the feature maps generated by BNNs are strongly constrained. To tackle this limitation, we propose the expanding-and-shrinking operation, which enhances binary feature maps with negligible increase of computation complexity, thereby strengthening the representation capacity. Extensive experiments conducted on multiple benchmarks reveal that our approach generalizes well across diverse applications ranging from image classification, object detection to generative diffusion model, while also achieving remarkable improvement over various leading binarization algorithms based on different architectures including both CNNs and Transformers.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Expanding-and-Shrinking Binary Neural Networks" (ES-BNN), a technique to improve the accuracy of binary neural networks (BNNs) by enhancing the representation capacity of binary feature maps. The core idea is to expand the input channels and shrink the convolution kernels in each layer while preserving the original computation complexity and parameter count. The authors combine this operation with binary group convolution to balance the channel expansion and kernel shrinkage. They demonstrate the effectiveness of ES-BNN across diverse tasks (image classification, object detection, generative diffusion models) and architectures (CNNs, Transformers). The proposed method consistently improves the performance of existing BNN algorithms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of expanding channels and shrinking kernels to maintain computational complexity while increasing representation capacity is a clever and seemingly straightforward, but potentially impactful idea. The combination with group convolution adds another layer of refinement. While channel expansion and kernel shrinkage are not entirely novel ideas in the broader deep learning context, their targeted application and specific implementation within the constrained setting of BNNs makes it novel.  The specific combination of expansion, shrinkage, group convolution, and its application to both CNNs and Transformers within binarized networks represents a solid incremental contribution.

*   **Significance:** BNNs are crucial for low-resource deployment.  Addressing the accuracy degradation of BNNs compared to real-valued networks is a significant challenge. ES-BNN presents a practical approach that consistently improves performance across various BNN algorithms and tasks. The results, showing substantial gains on benchmarks like ImageNet and PASCAL VOC, suggest the potential for wider adoption of BNNs in resource-constrained scenarios.  The fact that the method is applicable to CNNs, Transformers and a range of applications demonstrates its generalizability, further boosting its significance. The quantitative assessment of representation capacity and how ES-BNN increases diversification of features is a nice touch.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear and well-motivated:** The paper clearly explains the problem (limited representation capacity of BNNs), the proposed solution (ES-BNN), and its benefits (accuracy improvement with minimal overhead).
    *   **Strong empirical results:** The experiments are comprehensive, covering various tasks, datasets, and architectures. The consistently positive results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of ES-BNN.
    *   **Generalizability:** The applicability of ES-BNN to different BNN algorithms, network architectures (CNNs, Transformers), and downstream tasks showcases its broad applicability.
    *   **Practicality:** The minimal increase in computational complexity makes ES-BNN a practically viable solution for real-world deployment.
    *   **Good Analysis:** The ablation studies and hyper-parameter analyses enhance the understanding of the method's inner workings.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited theoretical justification:** While the paper provides an intuitive explanation for why ES-BNN works, a deeper theoretical analysis of its impact on the loss landscape or generalization bounds would strengthen the argument.
    *   **Incremental Contribution:** While clever, the proposed approach might be seen as incremental. The base concepts of channel expansion and group convolutions are already known. The novelty resides in its application within the particular niche of BNNs.
    *  **Limited Ablation on different Expansion strategies:** The authors tested combinations of expansion and group convolution. It might have been even better to test various expansion methods, perhaps learning a non-uniform expansion or adapting it in some way to each layer.

*   **Potential Influence:**  ES-BNN has the potential to become a widely used technique in the BNN community. Its simplicity, effectiveness, and generalizability make it an attractive option for researchers and practitioners looking to improve the accuracy of their BNN models. The method could encourage further research into architectural modifications that can enhance the representation capacity of BNNs.

*   **Justification for Score:**
    The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of BNNs by providing a practical and effective method to improve their accuracy. The strong empirical results, clear motivation, and generalizability of ES-BNN make it a significant advancement. However, the lack of a deep theoretical analysis and incremental nature of the core idea prevent it from receiving a higher score.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Building Instruction-Tuning Datasets from Human-Written Instructions with Open-Weight Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23714v1)
- **Authors**: Youmi Ma, Sakae Mizuki, Kazuki Fujii, Taishi Nakamura, Masanari Ohi, Hinari Shimada, Taihei Shiotani, Koshiro Saito, Koki Maeda, Kakeru Hattori, Takumi Okamoto, Shigeki Ishida, Rio Yokota, Hiroya Takamura, Naoaki Okazaki
- **Abstract**: Instruction tuning is crucial for enabling Large Language Models (LLMs) to solve real-world tasks. Prior work has shown the effectiveness of instruction-tuning data synthesized solely from LLMs, raising a fundamental question: Do we still need human-originated signals for instruction tuning? This work answers the question affirmatively: we build state-of-the-art instruction-tuning datasets sourced from human-written instructions, by simply pairing them with LLM-generated responses. LLMs fine-tuned on our datasets consistently outperform those fine-tuned on existing ones. Our data construction approach can be easily adapted to other languages; we build datasets for Japanese and confirm that LLMs tuned with our data reach state-of-the-art performance. Analyses suggest that instruction-tuning in a new language allows LLMs to follow instructions, while the tuned models exhibit a notable lack of culture-specific knowledge in that language. The datasets and fine-tuned models will be publicly available. Our datasets, synthesized with open-weight LLMs, are openly distributed under permissive licenses, allowing for diverse use cases.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the question of whether human-written instructions are still valuable for instruction tuning of Large Language Models (LLMs), given the success of LLM-generated instructions.  The authors create instruction-tuning datasets by pairing human-written instructions (sourced from the LMSYS-Chat-1M dataset) with responses generated by open-weight LLMs (Llama-3.1 and Gemma-2). They fine-tune several LLMs on these datasets and compare their performance to models fine-tuned on the state-of-the-art Magpie dataset (which uses LLM-generated instructions). The results show that LLMs fine-tuned on the human-written instruction datasets consistently outperform those fine-tuned on Magpie. The authors also create Japanese datasets using machine translation and analyze the cross-lingual transfer of instruction-following abilities. They find that instruction-tuning in a new language allows LLMs to follow instructions, but doesn't necessarily improve culture-specific knowledge. Finally, they make the datasets and fine-tuned models publicly available under permissive licenses.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in demonstrating the continued importance of human-written instructions for instruction tuning, even in the era of powerful LLMs. While instruction tuning itself is not novel, systematically comparing human-written versus LLM-generated instructions on publicly available LLMs and data is a valuable contribution. The creation of Japanese datasets and the analysis of cross-lingual transfer are also valuable additions, though less central to the core argument.

*   **Significance:** The finding that human-written instructions are superior or at least highly competitive with LLM-generated instructions is significant. It suggests that focusing solely on scaling LLM-generated data might lead to suboptimal results. The paper provides a practical recipe for creating high-quality instruction-tuning datasets by leveraging existing human-generated conversations and pairing them with responses from readily available open-weight LLMs. The open-source release of datasets and models further amplifies the paper's significance by enabling broader research and application.
    * It highlights the importance of diverse and high-quality data sources for training LLMs, beyond simply scaling the size of datasets.
    * It encourages further research into the optimal combination of human and machine contributions in creating instruction-tuning data.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Research Question:** The paper clearly defines its research question and provides a well-structured approach to answering it.
    *   **Rigorous Evaluation:**  The authors perform a comprehensive evaluation using a standardized benchmark (MT-Bench) and compare against strong baselines and state-of-the-art datasets.
    *   **Open-Source Contribution:**  The public release of datasets and models is a major strength, facilitating reproducibility and further research.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper provides insightful analyses of the results, including the cross-lingual transfer experiments and the breakdown by task category.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Machine Translation:** While the Japanese datasets are a valuable addition, the use of machine translation might introduce biases or artifacts that could affect the results. A dataset with native Japanese speakers directly writing the instructions could strengthen the conclusions.
    *   **Limited Diversity of Human Instructions:** The human instructions are extracted from LMSYS-Chat-1M, which might not fully represent the diversity of real-world user needs. Expanding the source of human instructions could improve the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Limited Scope:** Although valuable, the analysis of cross-lingual transfer and cultural nuances is somewhat shallow.  A more in-depth analysis of these aspects could further enhance the paper's impact.
    *   **Potentially confounding factors:** The responses generated by LLMs may reflect the pre-existing biases and limitations of the models themselves, which can influence the performance of models fine-tuned on these responses. It is hard to completely isolate the effect of human written prompts versus LLM generated prompts given this response generation architecture.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the direction of research in instruction tuning, encouraging a greater focus on the quality and diversity of human-written instructions. The open-source resources will also be valuable for researchers and practitioners working with LLMs.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**  The paper makes a significant and timely contribution to the field of instruction tuning by demonstrating the value of human-written instructions. The empirical results are compelling, the evaluation is rigorous, and the open-source release is a major strength. While there are some limitations regarding the use of machine translation and the diversity of the instruction sources, the paper's core findings are well-supported and have the potential to influence future research and development in the field. The novelty is good rather than outstanding, and the potential influence is solid.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### HOIGen-1M: A Large-scale Dataset for Human-Object Interaction Video Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23715v1)
- **Authors**: Kun Liu, Qi Liu, Xinchen Liu, Jie Li, Yongdong Zhang, Jiebo Luo, Xiaodong He, Wu Liu
- **Abstract**: Text-to-video (T2V) generation has made tremendous progress in generating complicated scenes based on texts. However, human-object interaction (HOI) often cannot be precisely generated by current T2V models due to the lack of large-scale videos with accurate captions for HOI. To address this issue, we introduce HOIGen-1M, the first largescale dataset for HOI Generation, consisting of over one million high-quality videos collected from diverse sources. In particular, to guarantee the high quality of videos, we first design an efficient framework to automatically curate HOI videos using the powerful multimodal large language models (MLLMs), and then the videos are further cleaned by human annotators. Moreover, to obtain accurate textual captions for HOI videos, we design a novel video description method based on a Mixture-of-Multimodal-Experts (MoME) strategy that not only generates expressive captions but also eliminates the hallucination by individual MLLM. Furthermore, due to the lack of an evaluation framework for generated HOI videos, we propose two new metrics to assess the quality of generated videos in a coarse-to-fine manner. Extensive experiments reveal that current T2V models struggle to generate high-quality HOI videos and confirm that our HOIGen-1M dataset is instrumental for improving HOI video generation. Project webpage is available at https://liuqi-creat.github.io/HOIGen.github.io.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "HOIGen-1M: A Large-scale Dataset for Human-Object Interaction Video Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HOIGen-1M, a new large-scale dataset specifically designed for training and evaluating text-to-video (T2V) models to generate videos with accurate human-object interactions (HOI). The dataset consists of over one million high-quality video clips, curated from diverse sources using an efficient framework based on multimodal large language models (MLLMs).  To ensure the captions are accurate, the authors propose a novel video description method based on a Mixture-of-Multimodal-Experts (MoME) strategy to eliminate hallucinations. Finally, the paper proposes two new metrics, CoarseHOIScore and FineHOIScore, to assess the quality of generated HOI videos. The paper demonstrates the limitations of current T2V models in generating HOI videos and highlights the effectiveness of HOIGen-1M in improving HOI video generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The creation of a large-scale, HOI-focused video dataset with carefully curated high-quality videos and captions is a significant contribution. While large-scale video datasets exist, they often lack the specific focus and quality of HOIGen-1M.  The MoME strategy for captioning to mitigate hallucination is also a notable innovation, leveraging multiple MLLMs for cross-verification. The proposed evaluation metrics, CoarseHOIScore and FineHOIScore, address the lack of specialized tools for assessing HOI generation quality.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial bottleneck in T2V research: the lack of high-quality, HOI-specific training data. HOIGen-1M has the potential to significantly advance research in this area, enabling T2V models to generate more realistic and accurate videos with complex interactions.  The evaluation metrics provide a means to quantitatively assess progress in HOI video generation. The extensive experiments demonstrating the limitations of current models underscore the importance of this dataset and the associated evaluation framework.  The work also lays the groundwork for further research on improving captioning strategies and evaluation methodologies for HOI video generation. The comparison to commercial software highlights the difficulty of the task, increasing the importance of their proposed benchmark.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Scale and Quality:** The size and verified HOI content of the dataset are major strengths.
    *   **Captioning Strategy:** The MoME captioning approach addresses the critical issue of hallucination in MLLM-based captioning.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** The introduction of CoarseHOIScore and FineHOIScore is valuable for assessing HOI quality in generated videos.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation of several existing T2V models, highlighting their limitations.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The data pipeline is complex.
    *   **Human verification:** although the authors use human verification to guarantee the high quality of the dataset, there are still bias and labor cost issues.
    *   **Limited scope:** The evaluation metrics, while novel, might not fully capture the nuances of human perception regarding interaction quality. The paper acknowledges the limitations, saying "there is still a disparity between these metrics and human preferences". This means the evaluation is not fully aligned with human assessment.
    *   **Dependence on External Tools:** The proposed metrics rely on existing HOI detectors and keypoint estimators, the performance of which can affect the reliability of the metrics.

*   **Potential Influence:** HOIGen-1M is likely to become a widely used benchmark in the field of T2V generation, particularly for HOI-related tasks. The captioning strategy and evaluation metrics can inspire further research in this area. The dataset could also have broader applications in areas like human activity recognition and robotics.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

While the paper demonstrates significant contributions, including a carefully curated, large-scale dataset and novel evaluation methods, some aspects could be improved. The paper's reliance on human verification, and the acknowledged disparity between proposed metrics and human preference, prevent it from achieving a higher score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Effective Cloud Removal for Remote Sensing Images by an Improved Mean-Reverting Denoising Model with Elucidated Design Space
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23717v1)
- **Authors**: Yi Liu, Wengen Li, Jihong Guan, Shuigeng Zhou, Yichao Zhang
- **Abstract**: Cloud removal (CR) remains a challenging task in remote sensing image processing. Although diffusion models (DM) exhibit strong generative capabilities, their direct applications to CR are suboptimal, as they generate cloudless images from random noise, ignoring inherent information in cloudy inputs. To overcome this drawback, we develop a new CR model EMRDM based on mean-reverting diffusion models (MRDMs) to establish a direct diffusion process between cloudy and cloudless images. Compared to current MRDMs, EMRDM offers a modular framework with updatable modules and an elucidated design space, based on a reformulated forward process and a new ordinary differential equation (ODE)-based backward process. Leveraging our framework, we redesign key MRDM modules to boost CR performance, including restructuring the denoiser via a preconditioning technique, reorganizing the training process, and improving the sampling process by introducing deterministic and stochastic samplers. To achieve multi-temporal CR, we further develop a denoising network for simultaneously denoising sequential images. Experiments on mono-temporal and multi-temporal datasets demonstrate the superior performance of EMRDM. Our code is available at https://github.com/Ly403/EMRDM.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EMRDM, a novel cloud removal model for remote sensing images based on improved mean-reverting diffusion models (MRDMs). It addresses the limitations of directly applying diffusion models (DMs) to cloud removal, where the inherent information in cloudy inputs is often ignored. EMRDM establishes a direct diffusion process between cloudy and cloudless images, offering a modular framework with updatable modules and an elucidated design space. The key improvements include a reformulated forward process, an ODE-based backward process, restructuring the denoiser with preconditioning, reorganizing the training process, and employing deterministic and stochastic samplers. The model is further extended to handle multi-temporal cloud removal. Experiments on both mono-temporal and multi-temporal datasets demonstrate the superior performance of EMRDM.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a moderately novel approach. The idea of using mean-reverting diffusion models for cloud removal isn't entirely new, as MRDMs have been explored for image restoration tasks (as cited by the authors). However, the specific modifications and the elucidated design space within the MRDM framework for cloud removal do offer a clear contribution. The modularity of the proposed framework is a significant strength, enabling future research to build upon and improve individual components.
* **Significance:** Cloud removal is a critical preprocessing step for various remote sensing applications, increasing the practical value of this research. The EMRDM model demonstrates superior performance compared to existing methods, indicating a tangible improvement in cloud removal quality. The multi-temporal aspect adds further value, as it addresses the need for consistent data across time series analyses. The fact that the work is accompanied by accessible code enhances its potential for adoption and further development by the remote sensing community.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Modular Framework:** The clarified design space and modular structure are a significant advantage.  The paper makes a clear effort to break down the MRDM process and highlight the role of each component.
    * **Performance:** The experimental results consistently demonstrate superior performance across multiple datasets and metrics.
    * **Multi-Temporal Extension:** The extension to multi-temporal cloud removal is valuable and practical.
    * **Clear Implementation Details:** The paper provides relatively comprehensive information on the implementation, training, and evaluation procedures, increasing its reproducibility.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques and the specific design choices are novel, the individual components are mostly based on existing work in diffusion models and image restoration.
    * **Limited Ablation Studies:**  While ablation experiments are included, they focus primarily on the overall modules. Deeper ablation of specific hyperparameter settings and architectural choices within the proposed attention or temporal modules could strengthen the analysis.
    * **Computational Cost:** While the paper mentions that their sampling times are suitable, a more thorough discussion of the computational complexity compared to alternative methods, and ways to minimize this complexity would strengthen the paper.

* **Potential Impact:** The EMRDM model has the potential to significantly improve the quality of cloud removal in remote sensing imagery. The modular framework could foster further research and development in this area. The open-source availability of the code will facilitate the adoption of this technique by other researchers and practitioners.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7.5** to this paper. While the core idea of using mean-reverting diffusion models isn't radically new, the EMRDM framework presents a significant engineering contribution by thoroughly dissecting and redesigning MRDMs specifically for cloud removal. The modular framework, the focus on an elucidated design space, the detailed implementation, and the demonstrated performance gains justify a score above average. However, a lack of extremely transformative new algorithmic ideas and scope for deeper analysis (particularly on computational cost and more granular ablation studies) prevent it from receiving a higher score.
Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### AdaMMS: Model Merging for Heterogeneous Multimodal Large Language Models with Unsupervised Coefficient Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23733v1)
- **Authors**: Yiyang Du, Xiaochen Wang, Chi Chen, Jiabo Ye, Yiru Wang, Peng Li, Ming Yan, Ji Zhang, Fei Huang, Zhifang Sui, Maosong Sun, Yang Liu
- **Abstract**: Recently, model merging methods have demonstrated powerful strengths in combining abilities on various tasks from multiple Large Language Models (LLMs). While previous model merging methods mainly focus on merging homogeneous models with identical architecture, they meet challenges when dealing with Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) with inherent heterogeneous property, including differences in model architecture and the asymmetry in the parameter space. In this work, we propose AdaMMS, a novel model merging method tailored for heterogeneous MLLMs. Our method tackles the challenges in three steps: mapping, merging and searching. Specifically, we first design mapping function between models to apply model merging on MLLMs with different architecture. Then we apply linear interpolation on model weights to actively adapt the asymmetry in the heterogeneous MLLMs. Finally in the hyper-parameter searching step, we propose an unsupervised hyper-parameter selection method for model merging. As the first model merging method capable of merging heterogeneous MLLMs without labeled data, extensive experiments on various model combinations demonstrated that AdaMMS outperforms previous model merging methods on various vision-language benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AdaMMS, a novel model merging method designed specifically for heterogeneous multimodal large language models (MLLMs).  AdaMMS addresses challenges arising from architectural differences and parameter space asymmetry between MLLMs.  The method comprises three steps: (1) defining a mapping function to align parameters across different architectures, (2) applying linear interpolation with an adaptive coefficient to manage parameter asymmetry, and (3) employing an unsupervised hyperparameter selection method based on generation consistency to optimize the interpolation coefficient for each task, without requiring labeled data.  Extensive experiments on various model combinations and vision-language benchmarks demonstrate that AdaMMS outperforms previous model merging methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its ability to perform model merging on *heterogeneous* MLLMs without relying on labeled data for hyperparameter tuning. Previous methods for MLLM merging either focused on homogeneous models or required additional training with supervision. The introduction of a mapping function to handle architectural differences and the use of generation consistency as a proxy for performance are also innovative contributions. The theoretical justification for the generation consistency approach adds to the paper's strength.

*   **Significance:** The work is significant because it unlocks the potential to combine capabilities from diverse MLLMs, leading to improved performance and more efficient model development. The unsupervised hyperparameter selection significantly reduces the reliance on labeled data and computational resources, increasing the accessibility of model merging techniques. The demonstrated improvements on vision-language benchmarks showcase the practical benefits of the proposed method.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined methodology with a three-step approach.
    *   Strong empirical results demonstrating improvements over baselines on various benchmarks.
    *   Theoretical justification for the generation consistency metric.
    *   Comprehensive experiments across different model combinations.
    *   Address a significant limitation in applying model merging to MLLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper shows that a subset of 100 inputs works well, it is not thoroughly analyzed how to select this subset, and if any selection biases could arise.

    *   The focus is specifically on MLLMs with limited architectural differences, i.e., variations in transformer block duplications. While significant, it doesn't address more fundamental architectural dissimilarities.

    *   Although the paper discusses its limitations, there is no deeper analysis or discussion in what other type of model merging that the framework could be extended to.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of MLLMs by enabling the fusion of capabilities from diverse models. This could lead to more powerful and versatile MLLMs, and it opens avenues for future research on unsupervised model merging and performance estimation.  The idea of using generation consistency as a proxy for performance could be applicable to other areas of machine learning.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field by addressing a key limitation in applying model merging to heterogeneous MLLMs. The combination of the mapping function, adaptive linear interpolation, and unsupervised hyperparameter selection demonstrates a well-thought-out approach that is both theoretically grounded and empirically validated. The work unlocks new possibilities for model fusion and improves the efficiency of MLLM development. However, the scope of the architectural differences addressed is somewhat limited, and there are no studies in the theoretical robustness of the used unsupervised hyper-parameter selection. While it addresses a clear and important challenge, the somewhat limited scope justifies a more modest score.
.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LANID: LLM-assisted New Intent Discovery
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23740v1)
- **Authors**: Lu Fan, Jiashu Pu, Rongsheng Zhang, Xiao-Ming Wu
- **Abstract**: Task-oriented Dialogue Systems (TODS) often face the challenge of encountering new intents. New Intent Discovery (NID) is a crucial task that aims to identify these novel intents while maintaining the capability to recognize existing ones. Previous efforts to adapt TODS to new intents have struggled with inadequate semantic representation or have depended on external knowledge, which is often not scalable or flexible. Recently, Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated strong zero-shot capabilities; however, their scale can be impractical for real-world applications that involve extensive queries. To address the limitations of existing NID methods by leveraging LLMs, we propose LANID, a framework that enhances the semantic representation of lightweight NID encoders with the guidance of LLMs. Specifically, LANID employs the $K$-nearest neighbors and Density-Based Spatial Clustering of Applications with Noise (DBSCAN) algorithms to sample selective utterance pairs from the training set. It then queries an LLM to ascertain the relationships between these pairs. The data produced from this process is utilized to design a contrastive fine-tuning task, which is then used to train a small encoder with a contrastive triplet loss. Our experimental results demonstrate the efficacy of the proposed method across three distinct NID datasets, surpassing strong baselines in both unsupervised and semi-supervised settings. Our code is available at https://github.com/floatSDSDS/LANID.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LANID, a novel framework for New Intent Discovery (NID) in task-oriented dialogue systems (TODS). LANID addresses the limitations of existing NID methods by leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) to enhance the semantic representation of lightweight NID encoders. It uses a combination of k-nearest neighbors and DBSCAN to sample utterance pairs and queries an LLM to determine relationships between them. The data generated from this process is then used to design a contrastive fine-tuning task, training a smaller encoder with a contrastive triplet loss. The experimental results demonstrate that LANID outperforms strong baselines in both unsupervised and semi-supervised settings across three NID datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Approach:** The core idea of using LLMs to guide and enhance lightweight NID encoders through contrastive learning is innovative.  It addresses the scalability issues associated with directly using LLMs for NID and the representation limitations of existing lightweight models. The paper provides a practical method to inject LLM knowledge into a smaller model for improved in-domain NID performance.
    *   **Data Sampling Strategies:** The paper offers two different unsupervised sampling strategies: KNN-based and DBSCAN-based, demonstrating that careful selection of utterance pairs can improve efficiency and performance. The combination of these strategies provides a robust solution.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** Extensive experiments are conducted on three benchmark datasets, showcasing the superiority of LANID over existing methods in both unsupervised and semi-supervised settings. This contributes strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed framework.
    *   **Practicality:** By focusing on lightweight encoders, the framework enhances the practicality of applying LLM knowledge to real-world TODS with resource constraints and potential privacy concerns.
    *   **Clarity of Presentation:** The paper clearly explains the LANID framework, the data sampling strategies, and the experimental setup.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** While the paper addresses the scalability of LLMs, it still relies on their accessibility and quality. Performance could be affected if the selected LLM (gpt-3.5-turbo) is unavailable or provides inconsistent annotations. The cost of querying the LLM is not explicitly addressed.
    *   **Prompt Engineering:** The prompt design for the LLM is described as requiring some manual effort, raising the question of how sensitive the overall performance is to prompt variations.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** While the paper compares different sampling strategies, a thorough ablation study on the individual components of the contrastive loss, the LLM used for pseudo-labeling and data sampling parameters would further strengthen the analysis.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Individual Components:** KNN, DBSCAN and Contrastive learning are relatively established techniques. The paper's novelty lies in the combination and application within the NID context, guided by LLMs. This is well described but the individual parts are not ground breaking.

*   **Significance:**

    *   LANID provides a practical method for bridging the gap between powerful LLMs and lightweight NID encoders. This is important for deploying NID in real-world environments with limited resources.
    *   The framework can be readily adapted to different NID datasets and settings, demonstrating its flexibility and potential for broader adoption.
    *   The exploration of different unsupervised data sampling strategies offers valuable insights for future research on contrastive learning in NID and related tasks.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a well-executed approach to a relevant and challenging problem in TODS. The novelty lies in the integration of LLM-guided contrastive learning with unsupervised sampling strategies for efficient NID. While the individual components are not entirely new, their combination and application in this context are significant. The extensive experimental results provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of LANID. Although the paper could benefit from more ablation studies and a more detailed discussion of the LLM-related costs, it represents a valuable contribution to the field. It is definitely a research work that would be useful to practitioners in industry and academia, thus its significance.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Short-video Propagation Influence Rating: A New Real-world Dataset and A New Large Graph Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23746v1)
- **Authors**: Dizhan Xue, Jing Cui, Shengsheng Qian, Chuanrui Hu, Changsheng Xu
- **Abstract**: Short-video platforms have gained immense popularity, captivating the interest of millions, if not billions, of users globally. Recently, researchers have highlighted the significance of analyzing the propagation of short-videos, which typically involves discovering commercial values, public opinions, user behaviors, etc. This paper proposes a new Short-video Propagation Influence Rating (SPIR) task and aims to promote SPIR from both the dataset and method perspectives. First, we propose a new Cross-platform Short-Video (XS-Video) dataset, which aims to provide a large-scale and real-world short-video propagation network across various platforms to facilitate the research on short-video propagation. Our XS-Video dataset includes 117,720 videos, 381,926 samples, and 535 topics across 5 biggest Chinese platforms, annotated with the propagation influence from level 0 to 9. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first large-scale short-video dataset that contains cross-platform data or provides all of the views, likes, shares, collects, fans, comments, and comment content. Second, we propose a Large Graph Model (LGM) named NetGPT, based on a novel three-stage training mechanism, to bridge heterogeneous graph-structured data with the powerful reasoning ability and knowledge of Large Language Models (LLMs). Our NetGPT can comprehend and analyze the short-video propagation graph, enabling it to predict the long-term propagation influence of short-videos. Comprehensive experimental results evaluated by both classification and regression metrics on our XS-Video dataset indicate the superiority of our method for SPIR.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new task called Short-video Propagation Influence Rating (SPIR), aiming to predict the long-term influence of short videos within a large propagation network.  To facilitate research in this area, the authors present the XS-Video dataset, a large-scale, real-world dataset containing comprehensive content and interactive information from the five largest Chinese short-video platforms. The dataset includes 381,926 samples from 117,720 videos across 535 topics.  Furthermore, they propose a Large Graph Model (LGM) called NetGPT, designed to bridge heterogeneous graph data with Large Language Models (LLMs) through a three-stage training mechanism.  Experiments demonstrate that NetGPT outperforms state-of-the-art baselines on the SPIR task, as evaluated by both classification and regression metrics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Task:** Defining the SPIR task is a valuable contribution. Moving beyond simple popularity prediction to a more comprehensive influence rating is a relevant and important direction for research in short-video analysis. The task considers a multi-dimensional view, incorporating different interactive indicators accumulated over time, which is a clear improvement over prior work.
*   **Large-Scale Dataset:** The XS-Video dataset is a significant asset to the research community.  The scale, the real-world nature, and the cross-platform aspect are all valuable.  The inclusion of various interactive indicators (views, likes, shares, comments, etc.) and comment content enhances the dataset's utility. The construction of a large propagation graph with 5.5 million nodes and 1.7 billion edges offers a rich resource for studying video propagation.
*   **NetGPT Architecture:** The proposed NetGPT model, which fuses GNNs and LLMs with a novel three-stage training mechanism, addresses a critical challenge in the field. Effectively bridging heterogeneous graph data with the reasoning capabilities of LLMs is a promising approach. The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the importance of different components of NetGPT.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes a thorough experimental evaluation with multiple baselines and metrics. The ablation studies further investigate the contribution of different model components. The analyses of short-term vs. long-term prediction and platform-specific convergence offer deeper understanding of the dataset and model behavior.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Generalizability:** The dataset focuses solely on Chinese short-video platforms. While valuable, this limits the generalizability of the findings to other regions and cultures.  The specific dynamics of these platforms might not translate directly to platforms in other countries.
*   **Complexity of the Task:**  While the comprehensive influence rating is a strength, it also introduces complexity.  Defining clear and objective criteria for the influence levels is challenging, and the reliance on human annotation introduces potential subjectivity. The analysis of short and long-term prediction highlights the difficulty of the proposed dataset and task that the long-term prediction performance needs improvements.
*   **Incremental Novelty in the Model:** Although the integration of LLMs with GNNs is a timely and relevant approach, the novelty within the model's architecture remains incremental. The main novelty within the architecture is a custom three-stage training mechanism.

**Significance:**

The paper contributes to the growing field of short-video analysis by introducing a new task, a large-scale dataset, and a novel model architecture.  The XS-Video dataset should stimulate further research on video propagation. The findings on the effectiveness of combining GNNs and LLMs for graph-structured data will be of interest to researchers working on graph neural networks and natural language processing.

**Justification for Score:**

While the dataset and task are significant contributions, the limited generalizability of the dataset and the incremental nature of the model's novelty prevent it from receiving a very high score. The approach is well-executed, and the empirical results are convincing. Overall, the paper represents a solid contribution to the field but doesn't fundamentally alter the landscape of research in short-video analysis.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### THEMIS: Towards Practical Intellectual Property Protection for Post-Deployment On-Device Deep Learning Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23748v1)
- **Authors**: Yujin Huang, Zhi Zhang, Qingchuan Zhao, Xingliang Yuan, Chunyang Chen
- **Abstract**: On-device deep learning (DL) has rapidly gained adoption in mobile apps, offering the benefits of offline model inference and user privacy preservation over cloud-based approaches. However, it inevitably stores models on user devices, introducing new vulnerabilities, particularly model-stealing attacks and intellectual property infringement. While system-level protections like Trusted Execution Environments (TEEs) provide a robust solution, practical challenges remain in achieving scalable on-device DL model protection, including complexities in supporting third-party models and limited adoption in current mobile solutions. Advancements in TEE-enabled hardware, such as NVIDIA's GPU-based TEEs, may address these obstacles in the future. Currently, watermarking serves as a common defense against model theft but also faces challenges here as many mobile app developers lack corresponding machine learning expertise and the inherent read-only and inference-only nature of on-device DL models prevents third parties like app stores from implementing existing watermarking techniques in post-deployment models. To protect the intellectual property of on-device DL models, in this paper, we propose THEMIS, an automatic tool that lifts the read-only restriction of on-device DL models by reconstructing their writable counterparts and leverages the untrainable nature of on-device DL models to solve watermark parameters and protect the model owner's intellectual property. Extensive experimental results across various datasets and model structures show the superiority of THEMIS in terms of different metrics. Further, an empirical investigation of 403 real-world DL mobile apps from Google Play is performed with a success rate of 81.14%, showing the practicality of THEMIS.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces THEMIS, an automatic tool designed to protect the intellectual property of on-device deep learning (DL) models against model-stealing attacks. Addressing the challenges posed by the read-only and inference-only nature of these models, THEMIS reconstructs writable counterparts and leverages the un-trainable characteristic to embed watermarks. It includes techniques for extracting encrypted models, creating writable models, and a novel training-free watermarking approach called Feed-Forward Knowledge Editing Watermarking (FFKEW). The paper presents extensive experimental results across various datasets and model structures, demonstrating THEMIS's superiority in terms of watermark success rate and accuracy, and an empirical investigation of real-world DL mobile apps from Google Play shows the practicality of THEMIS.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core problem addressed is novel and increasingly relevant. Existing watermarking methods aren't directly applicable to on-device, inference-only DL models deployed in mobile apps. The paper's key contributions: Model Rooting (reconstructing writable models), a data synthesis approach, and the FFKEW algorithm for watermark embedding are all significant advancements. Model Rooting, in particular, offers a creative way to circumvent the read-only nature of on-device models.

*   **Significance:** The threat of model stealing from mobile apps is a real concern for developers. THEMIS provides a practical, automated tool that allows app store administrators to protect the IP of these models, preventing unauthorized commercialization. The end-to-end evaluation on real-world apps significantly increases the paper's impact.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly articulates the problem and the limitations of existing solutions.
    *   **Technical Contributions:** The techniques presented (Model Rooting, FFKEW) are technically sound and well-explained. The data synthesis approach addresses a key challenge in watermarking inference-only models.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes both controlled experiments with benchmark datasets and an empirical evaluation on real-world apps, strengthening its claims.
    *   **Practical Focus:** The tool is designed to be used by app store administrators, addressing a practical need and filling a gap in available solutions.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Threat Model Limitations:** While the threat model acknowledges sophisticated adversaries, the tool's security hinges on access control at the app store level.  A more robust discussion of potential bypasses or collusion scenarios could strengthen the analysis. The dependence on the app store administrator's good faith, while arguably reasonable, isn't a perfect defense. A more thorough exploration of scenarios where app store security is compromised would be useful.
    *   **Reliance on Existing Backdoor Attacks:**  While FFKEW is novel, THEMIS still relies on established training-free backdoor algorithms. The novelty is more in *adapting* and *applying* these techniques to the specific constraints of on-device models rather than inventing fundamentally new algorithms. It should also be mentioned that while FFKEW outperforms its competitors in WSR and ACC, it also has a higher time of implementation.
    *   **Ethical Concerns and Misuse:** Although the paper briefly mentions ethical considerations, a more in-depth discussion would be beneficial. There are potential privacy implications of reverse engineering mobile apps and modifying on-device models, even with the stated authorization protocols.
    *   **Security Analysis:** While the experiments indicate a reasonable level of robustness to model extraction attacks, a more rigorous security analysis of the approach is needed. For example, what would be the effect of watermarking on models that have been already fine-tuned?

*   **Potential Influence:** THEMIS has the potential to significantly impact how on-device DL models are protected in the mobile app ecosystem. It could encourage wider adoption of on-device ML and reduce the risks for developers. The ideas presented could also inspire further research into model watermarking and security for edge devices.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper addresses a timely and important problem with a novel and practical solution. The technical contributions are significant, and the experimental evaluation is comprehensive. However, some limitations in the threat model and security analysis, as well as the adaptation rather than invention of watermarking techniques, prevent it from reaching a higher score.
It presents novelty in how it addresses this specific problem and provides promising results. The practical demonstration on real-world apps significantly strengthens the claims.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### StrokeFusion: Vector Sketch Generation via Joint Stroke-UDF Encoding and Latent Sequence Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23752v1)
- **Authors**: Jin Zhou, Yi Zhou, Pengfei Xu, Hui Huang
- **Abstract**: In the field of sketch generation, raster-format trained models often produce non-stroke artifacts, while vector-format trained models typically lack a holistic understanding of sketches, leading to compromised recognizability. Moreover, existing methods struggle to extract common features from similar elements (e.g., eyes of animals) appearing at varying positions across sketches. To address these challenges, we propose StrokeFusion, a two-stage framework for vector sketch generation. It contains a dual-modal sketch feature learning network that maps strokes into a high-quality latent space. This network decomposes sketches into normalized strokes and jointly encodes stroke sequences with Unsigned Distance Function (UDF) maps, representing sketches as sets of stroke feature vectors. Building upon this representation, our framework exploits a stroke-level latent diffusion model that simultaneously adjusts stroke position, scale, and trajectory during generation. This enables high-fidelity sketch generation while supporting stroke interpolation editing. Extensive experiments on the QuickDraw dataset demonstrate that our framework outperforms state-of-the-art techniques, validating its effectiveness in preserving structural integrity and semantic features. Code and models will be made publicly available upon publication.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "StrokeFusion: Vector Sketch Generation via Joint Stroke-UDF Encoding and Latent Sequence Diffusion" introduces a novel two-stage framework for generating vector sketches.  The first stage employs a dual-modal encoder that jointly learns features from vector strokes and Unsigned Distance Function (UDF) maps, resulting in a disentangled representation of stroke position, scale, and shape. The second stage utilizes a stroke-level latent diffusion model to generate the sketch in a non-autoregressive manner, simultaneously adjusting stroke position, scale, and trajectory. This approach aims to address limitations of existing methods, such as raster artifacts in raster-based models and lack of global understanding in vector-based models, while also enabling stroke-level control for editing. Experiments on the QuickDraw dataset demonstrate the superiority of the proposed framework compared to state-of-the-art techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of several techniques to address the limitations of existing sketch generation methods. The joint stroke-UDF encoding is a good idea to leverage both the vector and raster advantages. The non-autoregressive diffusion model for stroke generation is a useful contribution, addressing the limitations of sequence-based methods in handling unordered strokes. The disentangled learning of stroke attributes (position, scale, shape) is an important step towards controllable sketch generation. The work is a good combination of techniques that have already been proven out.

*   **Significance:** The proposed framework offers a significant advancement in vector sketch generation by enabling high-fidelity stroke generation with stroke-level control and improved structural integrity. The ability to learn position-invariant features and generate sketches in a non-autoregressive manner can have implications for various applications, including design, animation, and interactive prototyping. The performance gains demonstrated on the QuickDraw dataset further validate the effectiveness and potential of the proposed framework.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing sketch generation methods and motivates the need for a novel framework.
    *   **Technical Soundness:** The proposed framework is well-designed and technically sound, with a clear explanation of each component and their interactions.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes extensive experiments on the QuickDraw dataset, with both quantitative and qualitative comparisons against state-of-the-art baselines.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Ablation studies are conducted to evaluate the effectiveness of different components of the framework, providing insights into their contributions.
    *   **Stroke-level control is interesting**: The approach can be used for the controllable sketch generation

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Limitations:** The QuickDraw dataset is a simplified dataset of sketches, and the performance of the proposed framework may not generalize well to more complex and realistic sketches.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The dual-modal encoding and diffusion model may introduce significant computational overhead, which could limit its scalability and applicability in real-time applications.
    *   **Overhead of UDF Decoding:** The reliance on point sequences for stroke representation and the computational overhead of UDF decoding could be improved with more efficient representations.
    *   **Limited Generalization to Complex Sketches:** The model's performance is limited by its stroke-based approach and its inability to generate high-quality results when the input is from vector sketches with semantic features, such as an eye and hair being part of the same stroke.

*   **Impact:** The paper is well-written and the method is relatively easy to understand. The dual-modal encoding framework and the non-autoregressive diffusion model for stroke generation could inspire further research in sketch generation and other related areas. The ability to generate high-fidelity sketches with stroke-level control could have practical applications in design and animation tools.

    **Overall:** Despite the weaknesses, the paper presents a novel and technically sound framework for vector sketch generation with stroke-level control. The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework compared to existing methods. The potential impact of the work is significant, as it could lead to more powerful and versatile sketch generation tools.

**Score: 7.5**

*Rationale:*

The paper demonstrates a good understanding of the limitations of existing sketch generation methods and proposes a novel framework that addresses these limitations. While some of the individual techniques (e.g., diffusion models, transformers) have been explored in other contexts, the specific combination and application to vector sketch generation is novel. The extensive experimental results and ablation studies provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed framework. Given the limitations surrounding generalisability the work isn't a 'home run' paper, but it does represent a valuable contribution to the field, deserving of a score 7.5/10.

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Time-Series Forecasting via Topological Information Supervised Framework with Efficient Topological Feature Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.23757v2)
- **Authors**: ZiXin Lin, Nur Fariha Syaqina Zulkepli
- **Abstract**: Topological Data Analysis (TDA) has emerged as a powerful tool for extracting meaningful features from complex data structures, driving significant advancements in fields such as neuroscience, biology, machine learning, and financial modeling. Despite its success, the integration of TDA with time-series prediction remains underexplored due to three primary challenges: the limited utilization of temporal dependencies within topological features, computational bottlenecks associated with persistent homology, and the deterministic nature of TDA pipelines restricting generalized feature learning. This study addresses these challenges by proposing the Topological Information Supervised (TIS) Prediction framework, which leverages neural networks and Conditional Generative Adversarial Networks (CGANs) to generate synthetic topological features, preserving their distribution while significantly reducing computational time. We propose a novel training strategy that integrates topological consistency loss to improve the predictive accuracy of deep learning models. Specifically, we introduce two state-of-the-art models, TIS-BiGRU and TIS-Informer, designed to capture short-term and long-term temporal dependencies, respectively. Comparative experimental results demonstrate the superior performance of TIS models over conventional predictors, validating the effectiveness of integrating topological information. This work not only advances TDA-based time-series prediction but also opens new avenues for utilizing topological features in deep learning architectures.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper description:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Topological Information Supervised (TIS) Prediction, a novel framework designed to improve time-series prediction by integrating Topological Data Analysis (TDA). TIS addresses the limitations of traditional TDA-based prediction by utilizing neural networks and CGANs to generate synthetic topological features, preserving their distribution while reducing computational cost. A novel training strategy incorporates topological consistency loss to enhance the predictive accuracy of deep learning models. The framework includes two models, TIS-BiGRU and TIS-Informer, that capture both short-term and long-term temporal dependencies. Experimental results demonstrate superior performance compared to conventional predictors, highlighting the effectiveness of integrating topological information.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a potentially valuable contribution to the intersection of TDA and time-series prediction. The novelty lies in the following areas:

*   **Synthetic Topological Feature Generation:** Utilizing CGANs to generate synthetic topological features offers a significant advantage by mitigating the computational bottleneck associated with persistent homology calculations. This makes TDA more accessible for real-time or computationally intensive applications.
*   **Topological Consistency Loss:** Integrating a topological consistency loss during training helps ensure that the deep learning models learn representations that are aligned with the underlying topological structure of the data. This is a crucial step in ensuring that the learned features are meaningful and not just overfitting noise.
*   **Specialized Architectures (TIS-BiGRU and TIS-Informer):** Designing specific architectures tailored to capture short-term (BiGRU) and long-term (Informer) temporal dependencies demonstrates a nuanced understanding of time-series data and a thoughtful approach to model design.

However, a critical evaluation requires considering potential limitations and questions:

*   **CGAN Fidelity:** The success of the approach heavily relies on the ability of the CGAN to accurately capture and reproduce the distribution of the original topological features. The paper needs to clearly demonstrate and quantify the fidelity of the generated synthetic features. Are the generated persistence diagrams statistically indistinguishable from those computed directly from the data? What metrics are used to evaluate this fidelity?
*   **Generality of Topological Consistency Loss:** The effectiveness of the topological consistency loss might depend on the specific dataset and application. A more detailed explanation of how this loss is formulated and how it relates to the specific properties of the topological features is crucial. The paper needs to address potential scenarios where this loss might be less effective or even detrimental.
*   **Ablation Studies:** To rigorously validate the contribution of each component, the paper should include ablation studies that examine the impact of removing the CGAN-based feature generation or the topological consistency loss. This would provide stronger evidence for the value of each component.
*   **Comparison to Alternative Approaches:** The paper needs to clearly articulate how the proposed approach compares to other methods for accelerating TDA or integrating TDA into machine learning pipelines. Are there existing approximation techniques for persistent homology that are competitive? Have other studies explored differentiable TDA methods?
*   **Experimental Setup and Datasets:** The paper needs to provide more detailed information about the experimental setup, including the datasets used, the hyperparameters of the models, and the evaluation metrics. The choice of datasets should be justified, and the results should be compared to relevant baselines.
*   **Scalability and Practicality:** While the synthetic feature generation aims to improve scalability, the paper needs to discuss the computational cost associated with training the CGAN and the overall impact on training time. How well does the approach scale to very large datasets? Is the framework practically usable in real-world applications?
